On the mesher, before the workspace is archived, every file of 64 MiB or more goes to the exchange bucket on its own, already packed as the delivery bundle holds it - gzip members of its tar data at level 1, compressed on all the mesher's CPUs - and leaves the archive, which then carries only the small files (the worker used to compress the bundle itself: 93 minutes at level 9 for job 6cb2d606's 22 GB polyMesh). A file the worker's own checks read (a flow mesh's polyMesh) is fetched: unpacked to its place at collection. Each solver deck (`mesh.inp`, `.bdf`, `.cdb`, `.unv`) and each flow mesh solver file (`export_openfoam_case.zip`, `export_fluent.zip`, `export_ensight.zip`, `engines/cfd_exports.py`) of 256 MiB or more, and gmsh's own mesh (`mesh.msh`) of an FEA plan, is kept in the bucket instead; the worker only ever asks whether these are there. The bundle's big members - kept, or fetched and unchanged since - pass through as the mesher packed them; one the worker changed is packed again. Before gmsh's mesh goes, the mesher - which has the memory - writes the two light copies the worker reads in its place: the viewer's surface (`viewer_mesh.npz`, thinned face by face to at most 1.5M triangles with every face's outline kept) and a surface-only mesh with every named face for the reviewer (`review_surface.msh`, which the manifest then names). A record of what stayed (`kept_in_bucket.json`) rides home; the worker refuses one that names any object but this run's own. Delivery streams every download from the bucket into the artifact store - the solver files, gmsh's mesh, and the bundle, whose kept members pass through as stored - so none touches the worker's disk; the bundle is the same archive, byte for byte once unzipped, as one built from local files. The objects are deleted once the delivery is committed (the bucket's two-day lifecycle backs this up). An FEA pass kept for later shares its mesh files with the workspace (hard links), never a second copy. **Every result, flow or FEA, is collected under limits sized from what the mesher measured it sent** (the archive and the expanded total at 1.15 times its raw bytes, the largest file at 1.15 times its own, never past 48 GiB), and only when 85% of the worker's free disk holds it twice over (the archive, then the bundle) plus its files - else it is kept in the exchange for a worker with room, and said so. A cfMesh plan's machine is sized by the files its estimated cells will write (400 bytes a cell). Job 6cb2d606's 107M-cell cfMesh mesh (3.4 GB packed, 22 GB raw) was lost to the old cell-capped flow allowance (3.2 GB packed, 12.8 GB raw) after 44 minutes of meshing (`engines/bucket_files.py`, `adapters/mesh_execution/cloud_run_client.result_extraction_limits`). |
| RUN-8 | in force | **The size brake measures what the case was priced at, and only stops what it can make smaller.** The budget a snappy pass is braked against (RUN-5) is the plan's own cell budget plus the cells the gap boxes between an external body's parts raised snappy's ceiling by (PLAN-cfd-1: 13 cells across every gap, priced into maxGlobalCells by `snappy_runner._plan_body_gaps`) - those cells are asked for on purpose, so they are never read as a runaway (a slotted wing planned at 2M and raised to 6.44M for its gaps was stopped at 4.97M castellated cells and ended with no mesh; without the brake it meshed production-grade at 6.24M). A plan the gap boxes did not raise is braked exactly as before. And when the pass after a brake stop authors the very case the brake stopped, byte for byte (nothing left to cut: the gap boxes, the background mesh or the budget's floor price it back in), that case runs once more with the brake released (`engines/size_brake.release_brake`) - a run is never ended with no mesh for its size alone; the release is recorded in the why record with the count, budget and pass. |
| RUN-9 | in force | **A flow mesh's cell count picks its machine; the count is the user's** (`contracts/mesh_machine.for_cells` / `route_cells`; Areen, 14 Nov: "if the person writes 16 M in the notes, okay, move to the big machine; if they say 5 M, we don't; if they don't say anything, just mesh how we normally mesh"). A plan of up to `settings/policy.CELL_HARD_LIMIT` cells (8 M) runs on the standard machine, as always; a plan past it - on snappyHexMesh and cfMesh only ever the user's own count (CFDREQ-3), since our budgets stop at the standard machine - is written, before anything is meshed and with no question, to run on the smallest VM whose memory holds the mesher at `FLOW_GIB_PER_MCELL` (2.5 GiB a million cells, an estimate; RUN-3 steps a run that still needs more one machine up). A bigger machine already decided (a step up) is kept. The largest machine carries about 345 M cells; a count past it is the nearest, said. A sound mesh that comes out over the standard machine's cells is delivered as it is (LAD-cfd-4) - never thrown away to be re-meshed smaller - and its result is brought home at its own size, bounded by what the largest machine carries (`cloud_run_client.result_extraction_limits`). |

## The FEA builder's AI decisions (AI-fea-n): AI governs, rules guarantee

The builder model (the builder route) is shown the part as measured and the user's own words, and
answers ONE `fea_decision` tool call against a strict schema: no mesher command, no size. The rules
below turn each choice into exact sizes. The round goes through the AI decision cache, so the same
part with the same words gets the same decision and the same mesh. `FEA_AI=off` gives the rules'
own plan.

| ID | Status | Rule |
|---|---|---|
| AI-fea-1 | in force | **Refine** (the stress there matters to this user or this analysis): a hole, fillet or notch gets 10 elements per quarter-turn and 4 through the wall beside it; a wall 3 through it; a round 6 per quarter-turn (as a hot spot). Never finer than half the size the zone's own rule asks for; a sharp corner is never refined past its cap (its stress never settles). |
| AI-fea-2 | in force | **Coarser** (it does not matter for this analysis): a hole, fillet or notch keeps only its shape - 4 elements per quarter-turn and 2 through the wall, as any round (PLAN-fea-7); a sharp inside corner is capped at half its wall instead of a quarter. Walls and rounds are at their minimum already and stay. |
| AI-fea-3 | in force | **Small features.** Set aside: a hole, fillet or notch of radius at most 2% of the part's diagonal is meshed as coarser (AI-fea-2); a wall or anything larger is never set aside. Keep: a micro detail (PLAN-fea-12) at least a fiftieth of the base size gets a zone of its own size, so the mesh follows it; a smaller one stays set aside. |
| AI-fea-4 | in force | **An element count the user named is read by the AI** - only one they stated, in any words they wrote it (`1.2mil`, `half a million`, `700k-1.5M`, a count of nodes converted at about 1.5 nodes per 10-node element) - as `target_elements`, with `target_min` / `target_max` for a range or alone for a ceiling or a floor. The prompt names what is NOT a count: lengths and sizes (`2.5 m`, `3 mm fillets`, `2 mm elements`), bolt and thread sizes (`M8`), loads, pressures and stresses, densities per quarter-turn or through a wall. The rules check the answer: whole numbers between 1,000 and 10 billion, a range's minimum not over its maximum, a target outside its own range replaced by the range's middle; anything else is refused with a note and the rest of the decision stands. The count no longer lowers the element limit: the whole plan is scaled to it, both ways (PLAN-fea-27). There is no reading of the notes outside the AI: with the AI down, the decision saved for the same question answers (AI-fea-6); with none, the plan is the rules' own and the words and the result say plainly that an element count in the notes was not applied this run because the AI step did not answer. (Before 5 Nov the count was `budget_elements`, read the same way when a saved answer replays, and it only ever lowered the limit.) |
| AI-fea-5 | in force | **The minimums hold** after every choice: no choice leaves fewer than 2 elements through a wall, 4 per quarter-turn of a round or hot spot, or a corner coarser than half its wall. Then the plan is held to valid elements, the element, memory and time limits and every gate exactly as any plan is - over the limit, PLAN-fea-8 coarsens it to its validity minimums and names what is short. A zone the AI refined is checked on the mesh against its own finer target, so a refinement the mesh missed is named and pulls LAD-fea-3. The AI's refinements never cost the rules' own zones: when they would take the plan over its limit they are left out first (its other choices stand), and only then does PLAN-fea-8 coarsen. |
| AI-fea-6 | in force | **The AI never fails a run, and its availability never changes the mesh.** Unreachable, busy, slow (240 s, or what the attempt has left after 15 minutes kept for meshing, if less), no tool call, an answer the schema refuses: the decision saved for this same question (the AI decision cache: the same part, the same words, the same owner) is used when there is one; otherwise the rules' own plan. Either way it is said in one line and the why record names which. Off (`FEA_AI=off`): the rules' own plan. Choices on ids the model was not shown are dropped and recorded. |
| AI-fea-7 | in force | **Joints** (an assembly): bonded or contact per touching pair is shown as a suggestion for the user to confirm; it is never applied by itself. |
| AI-fea-8 | in force | **After a failed pass** (no mesh, or an invalid one) the AI may pick one lever from those the ladder allows for that failure (`engines/fea_ladder.lever_options`): the ladder's own first, the other unpulled fill lever, or LAD-fea-6. Any other answer: the ladder's own. |
| AI-fea-9 | in force | **Element sizes per part, read from the notes** (`engines/fea_part_sizes.py` on NOTE-4's shared reading). The phrases that ask for a size on particular parts ("an element size of around 3-4 mm for the frame rail, and 1.5-2 mm for the brackets, bolts and washers") are tied to the parts by the vision model and checked (NOTE-4), and become {part: [min, max]} for PLAN-fea-29. The model never gives a size of its own: it repeats the user's numbers from a quote of their words. No answer and none saved (AI-fea-6), FEA_AI off or no notes: nothing is applied and, with notes, said. Every entry, the mapping and the pictures are kept in the plan (`part_sizes`) and the result (`part_sizes`) for the console. |

The AI decisions are `engines/fea_ai.py`.

## MESH: a good file is never blocked by our own broken-CAD checks

Areen, 24 Oct: mesh first. A pre-mesh check that the CAD may be broken warns; only a mesh that then
fails at the flagged spot tells the user the part needs CAD repair. Detect only, never heal
(`cad/suspect_defects.py`, `application/fea_check.py`, `engines/fea_driver.py`).

| ID | Status | Rule |
|---|---|---|
| MESH-1 | in force | **Warn, then mesh.** Wherever a closed solid can be read (a CAD file with a solid body, or a triangle file whose surface closes), a face crossing another of its body, a paper-thin fold, and an open or invalid patch the clean check finds are WARNINGS on the stage, with the faces named - never a stop. The run meshes; a valid mesh is delivered with a note naming the spot. The only stops before meshing are a file nothing can be meshed from (a triangle surface that does not close, a CAD skin that nearly closes but for a gap, or nothing) and an unreadable file; a CAD file that is a surface is meshed as a shell (SURF-1, rulebook 59). A defect is CONFIRMED only when two independent measures agree (the fold's gap test or the crossing search, and OpenCASCADE's Boolean self-interference check on that solid alone naming the same faces); anything less is a note. **The checks are time-boxed** (rulebook 72, `cad/suspect_defects.find_bounded`): each runs in a child process under its own limit - the crossing search 300 s, each solid's Boolean check 60 s and 0.2 s a face of that solid, at most 300 s; no Boolean check of a solid no suspect lies on starts past 60 s - and a check past its limit is stopped and said as not checked (the run's note and its decision record), never a wait: a turbocharger's run spent minutes of "Measuring your part" in them, unbounded (17 Nov). The run shows them as their own step ("Checking for defects"). |
| MESH-1b | in force | **Closed shells stand for the file only when they hold the file** (`engines/fea_driver.solids_from_shells`, `SOLIDS_HOLD_SHARE`, `_held_share`). A file with no solid whose shells close is read as the solids they bound (MESH-1, SHELL-15j) only when those solids hold at least 98% of the file's face area. Fewer - a stamped sheet whose two bead pockets happen to close, 8 of its 73 faces and 7% of its area - is no reading of the file: it is the surface it is (`seen["open"]`, SURF-3) and every face is meshed; what closed is kept in `seen["partial"]`. Before, the pockets were staged as THE part and the sheet was dropped without a word (a 314-element fragment delivered as "passed"). Test: `tests/unit/engines/test_surface_run.py::test_a_sheet_with_a_small_closed_pocket_is_the_sheet_not_the_pocket`. |
| UNIT-IGES-1 | in force | **An IGES file is read in the unit its own flag declares** (`cad/unit_evidence._iges_evidence`, `_IGES_FLAGS`). A well-formed IGES unit the platform has no name for (flag 4 feet, miles, kilometres, mils, microns, microinches) is converted by OpenCASCADE at the file's own factor as it reads, so its output is true millimetres: the evidence is millimetres, "declared in the file as ft (304.8 mm), converted to millimetres as it is read" - as for STEP. Before, the unit was "unsupported", millimetres were assumed and OCC's conversion was undone as if it had been metres: a 2 m Maxsurf hull in feet (lab_sh0084, coordinates to 6.57 ft) staged at 2 mm on the website while the lab read 2,004 mm. Test: `tests/unit/cad/test_declared_foot_scale.py::test_an_iges_file_stages_at_its_declared_size`. |
| SHMESH-12b | in force | **A size larger than the whole part is said, with the way on** (`engines/gmsh/shell_adapter._mesh_once_core`, `SAID`, `said_words`, `too_small_words`; `engines/surface_run.run`, `engines/shell_run.run`). When every face of the part is under the smallest face the size meshes (SHMESH-12's sliver test), the run stops with the user's words - the part's size, the element size asked, a size that fits (a tenth of the part), and the file's unit to check - and the run's record says them as they stand. Before: "the shell mesh could not be made (ValueError: max() arg is an empty sequence)". Test: `tests/unit/engines/test_surface_run.py::test_a_size_larger_than_the_whole_part_is_said_in_plain_words`. |
| SHELL-18m | in force | **The shell is made on the part in millimetres** (`engines/shell_run.main`, `in_millimetres`, `MM_COPY`). The FEA driver stages the part in metres and runs the shell "staged"; the mid-surface extraction and the shell mesher are built and proven on millimetre geometry (every lab and scoreboard run hands them millimetres). The staged solid is written scaled to millimetres (staged_mm.brep, faces in the same order) and the run takes it at 1 mm per unit; its files are in metres as before. Before: a thin casting (cen_r0033_part2_hs_enclosure) read in metres kept 0.18 mm gaps and 0.22 mm edges its millimetre reading has not, and the website made 2,303 elements where the lab made 3,815. Test: `tests/unit/engines/test_shell_run.py::test_a_part_staged_in_metres_is_shelled_in_millimetres`. |
| MESH-1c | in force | **A shell mesh covers the part** (`engines/shell_delivery.coverage`, `mesh_area_mm2`, `kept_share`, `write_kept`, `KEPT_FILE`, `COVER_SHARE`, `cover_words`; recorded by `engines/shell_run.run` and `engines/surface_run.run` as the record's `coverage`; `engines/fea_driver.solids_from_shells` writes `KEPT_FILE`). Every delivered shell is measured: the elements' own area (a quad as two triangles) against the area the shell should have - a surface file's face area as uploaded, or for a solid its mid-surface's, taken as half the solid's face area (two skins a wall apart) less the regions already told as left out (SHSTAGE-9). When the FEA driver stages the file again holding less than the upload (MESH-1), the share it still holds is written beside it and the shell is measured against the upload. Under `COVER_SHARE` (0.9 of a surface, measured exactly: good runs 0.993-1.007; 0.3 of a solid's mid-surface, an estimate whose rims count in the half: good runs down to 0.43) the result is never a pass: a concern, a `part_covered` row and the first words say how much of the part has no elements. The net under MESH-1b for any path that drops faces before or inside the mesher: the old s33 reading (8 of 73 faces) reads 7% covered, not "passed"; two stamped lab domes (s21, s27) whose main face has no elements read 40% and 54%. Tests: `tests/unit/engines/test_surface_run.py::test_a_shell_that_covers_a_fraction_of_the_part_is_never_a_pass`, `tests/unit/engines/test_shell_run.py::test_the_shell_says_how_much_of_the_part_it_covers`. |
| MESH-2 | in force | **Needs CAD repair only where the mesh failed.** When every pass fails and the faces the mesher failed on (gmsh's own error, or the invalid elements) are faces a check flagged, the user is told the part needs CAD repair, with those faces named; the run is not taken again. A failure anywhere else is the ordinary FEA outcome. |
| MESH-3 | in force | **Zero false stops, gated.** Every broken-CAD check runs over the known-good files (`devtools/quality/broken_cad_gate.py`): CI holds the fixture set to zero stops on every change, and any change to a check runs the whole corpus (the clean CFD corpus's gate tier, the FEA corpus dev and holdout - presence and verdict only - Areen's files and the assembly corpus A set) on a lab VM, which must show zero stops. |
| MESH-4 | in force | **Meshed at the CAD's own scale when metres fail.** The staged solid is in metres, so OpenCASCADE's fixed tolerances (1e-7 of a model unit) are a tenth of a micron: a CAD edge of a few nanometres where one feature grazes another (libredrive_pulley_housing: a 1.8 mm half-hole touching a 28 mm bore, edges of 24 nm, 56 um and 24 nm) falls inside them, and gmsh stops ("Impossible to mesh periodic surface", "overlapping facets", a volume left empty). When the metre build of a CAD solid fails in the surface stage or the fill, the same file is read again at millimetre scale (the same entities and tags, every length of the plan or spec times 1000), meshed and curved there - every CAD curve shorter than PLAN-fea-12's micro length (a thousandth of the part's diagonal) as one segment, since gmsh's two segments on a nanometre curve put a node on its chord and fold the face beside it - and the nodes are scaled back to metres; quality.json says `fea_mesh_scale: mm` and how many curves were one segment. Only after a failure: a solid that meshes in metres meshes exactly as before. FEA adapter and the CFD gmsh path's CAD solids alike; a failure at both scales names its surfaces for LAD-fea-6 and MESH-2, the 2D stage's own error included. |
| MESH-5 | in force | **A face's repeated triangles are dropped before the fill.** gmsh's periodic mesher can lay the same three nodes down twice where a seam runs into a pole (nakuja_rocket's nose cone, Part_2_Face_5: an interior node placed just across the seam), and the fill refuses the boundary ("Found two duplicated facets", "overlapping facets on surface 5 surface 5") whatever the 2D algorithm or the scale. After the surface stage, each face keeps one copy of any triangle it repeats - a copy adds no surface - and none when the pair is a flap (an edge of it bounds no other triangle of the part's surface: a fold out and back over nothing, which would leave a triangle no element has as a face), and the fill goes on; a curved element the seam bends wrong is straightened by PLAN-fea-11. A face with no repeat (every mesh the fill accepts) is untouched. quality.json names the faces and copies. The CFD gmsh path does the same in its MESH-4 retry. |
| MESH-6 | in force | **Each CAD entity consults only the size fields that can size it.** gmsh reads the background size at every point it places, and the smallest-of field evaluates every field there - each Distance field a nearest-point search. A large assembly's plan makes hundreds: ox_mc_lid (253 parts, 545 zone groups) made 2,291 fields, 753 of them Distance fields over 22.9M sample points, and gmsh spent 2.0 s on each of its 18,229 curves - about 10 hours before the first surface (lab, 26 Oct). Before meshing, every field's lowest and highest possible value over each CAD entity is read from boxes (the entity's, and its sources' or balls', both widened by 5% of their own size plus 1e-4 of the model's): a Threshold is at least its value at the least distance between the boxes and at most its value at the farthest (each source holds a sample point), a thin spot's Max at least the larger of that and its balls'. The entity's ceiling is the smallest of the base size, a part's own base where its Constant field holds (its volumes and what bounds them) and every field's highest there. A field whose lowest is above the ceiling (by 1e-6 of it) can never be the smallest on that entity, so it is wrapped in a Restrict field that skips it there. Every size gmsh reads is the same number as before, to the bit, so every mesh is the same mesh - sooner. ox_mc_lid's curves then consulted 11 fields each (the median) instead of 1,006. FEA plans only; the CFD gmsh path has one field. |
| MESH-7 | in force | **Parts meshed apart are each filled with only their own surface mesh in the model.** gmsh's Delaunay reads the surface mesh of the whole model for every volume it fills - each boundary node's size from every face's triangles, and a search structure over every face's triangles to sort the tetrahedra - so filling a tied or contact assembly part by part costs the parts times the whole surface mesh: ox_mc_lid's 253 parts beside 4.9M surface elements spent about 30 s a part before a single tetrahedron (lab, 26 Oct). While one volume is filled every other surface's triangles are set aside; after the last fill each is put back as the fill left it (same elements, tags, order and orientation) and the nodes and elements are numbered as gmsh numbers them after a fill. A part's boundary nodes are its own, so each fill reads exactly what it read before: the same mesh, to the byte (6 small volumes beside 1.4M surface triangles: 13.9 s -> 2.0 s). Bonded parts fused into one boundary are filled in one call, as before. |
| MESH-8 | in force | **A skin of no thickness is left out of the mesh** (26 Oct, `cad/zero_skins.py`). A face lying on another face of the same solid, the other side out, with no material between them - a hole or pocket cut exactly to a wall, its floor left on top of that uncut wall - is a skin: every sample point of it lies on the other face's surface and inside its boundary, with opposite normals, and the two share no edge. No mesher can fill a solid that crosses itself so (job bf863215's tube: gmsh's "PLC Error: A segment and a facet intersect", alone and fused, at every size). Each part of an assembly is meshed with its skins left out and their outlines opened through the face they lay on, so the opening goes through; every other face keeps its CAD name, the decision names the skins, and the delivery says so. Nothing with material changes: the opened part is kept only when it is a valid closed solid of the original's volume, else the part is meshed as drawn and the ladder goes on (the fTetWild rung, LAD-fea-9, fills a part that crosses itself). The CAD file is never altered. **Every skin is accounted for** (27 Oct): the sidecar records each skin face with the face it lies on ("Part_7_Face_1 lies on Part_7_Face_5: zero thickness, no volume"), and every solver file's notes and the deck record list them. The measured face check (`engines/fea_review.face_accounting`, written to quality.json as `fea_faces`) counts a skin left out - like a tiny face MESH-9 merged into its neighbour - as accounted for, never as a missing named face; the reviewer's facts and the result screen say where each went. A review finding on the face count that this measurement settles is recorded as passed, and an FEA mesh the measurements call valid passes its review: its points stand as concerns (VER-4, `final_result.limits_decide_for`). Job fb44fed7, a valid 11-part mesh, read "FAIL" over 142 named faces against 146 - its 4 skins. |
| MESH-9 | in force | **A face or edge far below the element size there is meshed as part of its neighbour.** Ansys meshes through features at or under its Defeature Size, by default 0.005 x the element size (Meshing Options: "Mechanical Defeature Size Factor (Default: 0.005)", the CFD one the same), and its virtual topology merges slivers and short edges into their neighbours; gmsh meshes every face as drawn, so a strip a few microns wide pins slivers on and beside it. At mesh time, on the model only (the CAD file is never changed), a face whose width (twice its area over its perimeter) is under 0.005 of the smallest element size the plan can put on it or its neighbours (the FEA plan's zones read the way MESH-6 bounds them; on the CFD gmsh path the clamp size, its curvature sizing and the passage field) is meshed as one gmsh compound surface with one neighbour: a smooth one first (the faces' normals within 30 degrees, the feature angle the snappy path uses), the one it shares the most boundary with; with none, as Ansys's mesh defeaturing drops a feature at or under its Defeature Size whatever the angle, the sliver COLLAPSES onto its edge with the neighbour across the crease it shares most with (the corner moves by less than the sliver's width; quality.json says collapsed, and the crease). Never into another part (both faces bound the same solids), never into or out of a face of a joint between parts (bonded, tied or contact), never a face a stress hot spot names (PLAN-fea-4: a hole, fillet or notch; a corner PLAN-fea-5 caps is not one, its stress never settles and a sliver drawn there is the corner), and on the CFD path a face that is its named group's only face never goes into another group (a neighbour in its own group comes first). The curves left between the same two faces either side of a vertex that now joins only them are one compound curve where one of them is SHORT (under 0.005 of the local size) or the vertex was a corner of a merged face (and the curves run on within 30 degrees); the faces those curves bound are compounds too. gmsh puts every triangle back on the CAD face it lies on; every node of a compound goes back exactly onto its CAD face or curve before the fill, the quadratic midside nodes there onto the CAD at their edge's middle, and after the mesh a merged face's elements join its neighbour's named face, so a load on the neighbour covers both areas; quality.json (FEA `fea_tiny`, CFD `tiny_faces`) names each merge (which face into which, its width, the local size, and whether it collapsed across a crease) and the short edges meshed through. A part with nothing that small is untouched, to the byte. A build the merges break, on a face a merge meshes, is built again in the same pass with every face as drawn. Faces are named by their own CAD tag (Face_k = CAD face k), so a merged face that holds no triangle renames nothing. Not on the fTetWild rung (LAD-fea-9), not on staged, classified or external CFD surfaces, and not on snappyHexMesh or cfMesh, which mesh a triangulated surface and resolve features by their own angle. |
| MESH-10 | in force | **No optimiser leaves a volume worse than it got it** (27 Oct, `engines/gmsh/optimise_guard.py`). Every volume's mesh is read before gmsh's volume optimiser (Netgen) runs, on the FEA and the CFD gmsh path alike. A volume the optimiser leaves with more invalid elements (SICN at or below zero, straight sides) than it went in with gets its input mesh back - its own inside nodes and elements; the optimiser never moves a boundary node - and the run names it (`fea_optimiser_kept_input` / `optimiser_kept_input` in quality.json). Every other volume keeps the optimiser's work, trades included. A guard: Netgen splits every element it counts illegal (all four corners on the boundary - a quarter of a 2 mm plate's, on ox_mc_lid) and nothing checked what it handed back; on ox_mc_lid it handed back valid elements (the plates' inverted elements were PLAN-fea-11's). A run where the optimiser makes nothing invalid is exactly what it was before this rule. |
| MESH-23 | in force | **The surfaces are meshed on every core, the same mesh on any number** (`engines/gmsh/fea_parallel2d.py`, rulebook 49). The curves are meshed once, on one thread. The surfaces are then cut into fixed blocks - at most 64 runs of consecutive surface numbers of about equal work, read from the curve mesh alone, never from the number of workers - and each block is meshed by ONE gmsh call (only it visible) on one thread from gmsh's fixed seed, in forked worker processes holding the same model, fields and curve mesh; the parent puts every surface's nodes (with their parameters on the face) and triangles back in surface order, the curve nodes mapped curve by curve, and numbers nodes and elements as gmsh does after a stage. A block's mesh depends on that block alone, so the mesh is the same, to the byte, on 1 or N workers and run twice. It is not gmsh's old one-call surface mesh to the byte: gmsh draws one random stream for a whole call, so a surface's triangles depend on every surface meshed before it in that call (lab, 1 Nov: the first 15 of a 1,478-face plate's surfaces the same, 1,428 not), and no process can draw the numbers a surface it did not mesh would have drawn. Not used where surfaces cannot be meshed apart - a periodic face, MESH-9's compounds, the last rung's part-only pass, a pass meshing only some parts (LAD-fea-11's kept parts held aside) - which keep gmsh's one-call stage. Lab, 1 Nov: the 12 largest training parts (133k-7.7M elements) built byte-identical solver files on 1 worker process and twice on 8; the 12 textbook cases gave identical answers on 1 core and on 8; six 1,000-1,500-face parts gave byte-identical surface meshes on 1, 2, 4 and 8 workers. On 8 cores the surface stage ran 1.3-1.8 times sooner on those parts, and 1.26 times on the 640 mm part, where one face gmsh switches to MeshAdapt by itself takes most of the stage (a block is meshed by one process). The surface triangles differ slightly from main's one-call stage: on the 260-part corpus, the same parts delivered, the same outcomes and checks, element counts within 2.5%. |
| MESH-24 | in force | **A CAD edge far shorter, or a face far narrower, than the elements beside it gets elements of its own size there** (2 Nov, `engines/gmsh/fea_short.py`). Both ends of every CAD edge are mesh nodes, so an edge a few hundredths of a millimetre long beside elements sized at half a millimetre pins its two end nodes inside triangles and tetrahedra ten or more times longer than that: slivers no optimiser can move, since the nodes are the CAD's own vertices (the C5 knuckle, demo dry run 2 Nov: three fillets running out 0.027-0.030 mm before a corner beside 0.5 mm elements - worst scaled Jacobian 0.0029, aspect 153.6, dihedral angles 1.6 and 176.8 degrees; and a 0.00033 mm edge where two seams meet). At mesh time (FEA plans, not the fTetWild rung), every CAD edge shorter than 0.1 of the smallest element the plan can put on the faces it bounds (read off the plan's zones as MESH-6 bounds them), and every face narrower than that (twice its area over its perimeter, held against itself and its neighbours), gets a gmsh Distance field on itself and a Threshold: 1.5 times its length (width) within that length (width) of it, then growing at the plan's rate to the base size, as every zone grows - as Ansys and Abaqus resolve such an edge with a local edge sizing. Edges and faces MESH-9 meshes through (compounds) are left to it; a curve of no length (a pole, under a millionth of the base size) or one bounding a single face (a seam) is never sized. A narrow face and the short edges that bound it are one unit, sized together or left together (an end edge sized without its strip grades the strip's slivers into worse ones). THE BUDGET: the elements the fields add are estimated at the plan's density (per_h3) - a graded ball round an edge, a graded tube along a face - and may come to 25% of the plan's estimate (at least 25,000); past it the units smallest against their neighbours' size are sized first and the rest left as drawn. NEVER WORSE: when a sized mesh still misses an element-quality limit (the floor, scaled Jacobian, Jacobian ratio, aspect or dihedral rows of VER-2), or was not built, the same pass is meshed again with every short edge as drawn and the better of the two is kept - fewer of those rows missed, then the better worst scaled Jacobian (FEA corpus, 3 Nov: 5 of 21 sized parts kept as drawn; that costs those parts a second mesh). quality.json `fea_short_edges` counts the edges and faces sized, the shortest and narrowest, how many the budget left, and `kept: as drawn` with why when the as-drawn mesh won. A part with nothing that short has no new field: the same mesh, to the byte. |
| MESH-25 | in force | **An FEA solid's volume is filled by HXT on every core the job has** (rulebook 70, `engines/gmsh/fea_adapter.mesh`). The founder's decision, 13 Nov: speed wins over the same mesh twice. An FEA pass fills its volume with gmsh's HXT (Mesh.Algorithm3D 10) on every core the job has (its CPU quota, never the host's; the plan's own `threads` when it names them) instead of Delaunay. That is the only change: the curves and surfaces are meshed exactly as before (one thread, MESH-23's worker processes for the surfaces), and the shell route, the CFD gmsh path, staging and the surface files run gmsh on one thread as before, to the byte. On more than one thread the fill may differ slightly between runs; it is still held to every limit. One thread (`MESH_GMSH_THREADS=1`) gives the same mesh to the byte: OUR tests and sweep tools run that way, production never does. **HXT fills once.** A mesh that comes back is the pass's mesh, judged and laddered as any pass is - it is never meshed again with Delaunay to compare (the owner, 14 Nov). **Never a failed pass for it**: should the fill fail - an error, a volume left empty (`EMPTY VOLUMES`), no mesh, an invalid one (exit 4) - the same pass is filled again at once with Delaunay, exactly as every pass was filled before, and that outcome stands whatever it is; so no pass fails that Delaunay would mesh (corpus, 14 Nov: 4 of 186 parts, HXT leaving a volume empty, each delivered by Delaunay in the same pass). The mesher's log is kept across the two. *A size held through a volume* (a part's own base in an assembly, PLAN-fea-17) is not read by HXT - the inside came out 1.6 times coarser than the base - so such a model is filled by Delaunay from the start. quality.json `fea_algorithm3d` says which algorithm made the mesh, on how many threads, and why. LAD-fea-2 now puts Delaunay first (HXT after it). Corpus (lab, 14 Nov, 186 FEA parts at 8 vCPU each against the same code on Delaunay, run with a compare step since dropped): the same parts delivered, the 3D step 1.38 times sooner; HXT makes about 10% fewer elements for the same plan. |
| MESH-26 | in force | **An IGES boundary curve that turns a corner inside itself is cut at that corner, and only there** (rulebook 81; only at the corner since rulebook 89; `cad/iges_read.split_corner_faces`, `CORNER_TURN_DEG`). The product reads IGES with B-splines kept whole (`read.iges.bspline.continuity` 0, TIME-9's speed), so a boundary curve with C0 breaks (an inner knot of full multiplicity) is one edge. Where the curve runs on through a break - tangent, only its parameter speed jumping, or turning by 0.1 degree or less - that is right and fast, and the break stays inside the edge. Where it TURNS there by more than 0.1 degree it is a corner, and after the read that edge is cut at that break and at no other: each piece is the edge's own 3D curve and its own curves on its faces over the piece's span (no curve is re-made, no face's surface touched), with a new vertex at the corner; an edge two faces share is cut once, for both. A piece whose 3D curve and curve on a face lie further apart than the edge's tolerance over its span (BRepCheck samples a short piece closer than the whole edge) gets a tolerance that covers that gap. Every other break, edge and face is left exactly as read; a cut that would leave invalid a face that was valid as read is not made (logged). Every product read of an IGES gets this shape (`iges_reader()`'s OneShape and Shape, and the BREP handed to gmsh). The turn is the curve's own geometry, never a per-file switch. Before (rulebook 78), a public pipe whose four faces are each bounded by one closed curve through four right angles no longer sewed shut (read as open, 95 s in `solids_from_shells`; main: closed in 0.9 s), and two public ribbons bounded by polylines (1.7-2 degree turns) lost their 6.72 mm wall to re-approximated boundaries and were read as a skin; with the cut they read as on main (closed; a 411-element shell, wall 6.72 mm). Rulebook 81-88 instead split, by OpenCascade's divide, EVERY break of each cornered face's curves and of every face sharing any broken edge with one, turning or not: a 231-face spline export went from 1,340 edges to 8,262, its preparation from 13 s to 37 s, and its shell mesh no longer finished in 15 minutes; on the 123-file public corpus it multiplied the edges of 6 files by more than 1.5 (a one-face hull bow 4 -> 44, a pipe 20 -> 148), where the cut gives 4 -> 5 and 20 -> 32 (one edge more per corner; the two ribbons stay at 252, their 240 polyline corners all real), and the 115 files with no corner are left exactly as read. Measured on the 123-file public IGES corpus: every break that turns at all turns by 0.19 degrees or more (8 files), every other break by under 0.031 degrees (the thousands on the spline exports). Test: `tests/unit/cad/test_iges_read.py`. |
| MESH-mmg-1 | in force | **What gmsh's optimiser leaves, MMG improves inside the volume, the surface frozen** (`engines/fea_mmg.py`). After gmsh fills a part and its Netgen optimiser runs, and before the elements are curved, each volume that still holds a straight element a delivered quadratic mesh would be noted on (aspect over 20, a dihedral angle under 5 or over 170 degrees, a scaled Jacobian under 0.01 - the VER-2 rows of `engines/fea_quality.limits_for(2)`) goes through mmg3d (MmgTools/mmg v5.8.0, LGPL-3.0, built pinned in the mesh image) with `-nosurf -optim -hgrad -1`: it swaps, collapses, splits and moves inside the volume at the sizes the mesh already has, and every boundary triangle and boundary node is also marked required. Only the elements within two layers of shared corners of a flagged one may change (every other element is required and comes back as it went in), and mmg's answer is taken back cavity by cavity: a group of free elements is replaced only when what mmg made of it is better by the rule below and fills the same volume. The result is kept only when the boundary comes back as the same triangles on the same nodes (so every CAD face, named group, bonded or tied joint and the CAD fit are unchanged), every element is valid, none of the worst figures (scaled Jacobian, aspect, smallest and largest dihedral) is worse, no more elements miss a limit, and at least one figure is better; otherwise the gmsh mesh stays, to the byte. **The user's own limits are targets too** (PLAN-fea-34, rulebook 67): an aspect ratio (longest over shortest edge) or a smallest or largest corner angle the notes set flags the elements past it as well, and a cavity is kept only when, beside the rule above, no more elements are past the user's limits than before (fewer counts as better); `fea_mmg.targets` and each volume's `user_past` record it, and the requirement's row says what the pass did. A part with no such element is untouched; with no such limit in the notes the pass is exactly the one above, to the byte. Not on the fTetWild rung (LAD-fea-9) or a volume with embedded entities. mmg3d is single-threaded and deterministic. quality.json `fea_mmg` records each volume tried, its figures before and after, the time and why it was kept or not; FEA_MMG=off turns it off, and an image without mmg3d skips it and says so. The root cause of a sliver - a CAD detail far below the element size - is fixed in the geometry first (MESH-9, PLAN-fea-12); this pass is the safety net for what is left. |

## NOTE: the user's own notes

Areen, 26 Oct: customers often know things that help the mesh (an NDA customer's STEP came with a
spec sheet). The intake asks once, keeps their words exactly as typed, and every AI step on every
route reads them as guidance (`contracts/user_notes.py`). They are not acceptance criteria.

| ID | Status | Rule |
|---|---|---|
| NOTE-1 | in force | **Asked once, kept word for word.** Before the first summary the application asks one optional question ("Anything specific about this simulation we should know? ... Say 'no' to skip."), unless the user already said something specific. It is never asked again and never holds a submission. The user's answer, every message the intake quotes as saying something specific, and the console's notes box are stored verbatim on the session (`user_notes`), never as a model's rewrite; the summary (request_txt) stays the intake's own. A click on a button the application offered under its own message (`application/purpose_map.choices_for`) is never kept as a note: its words are ours (the founder's "Keep 8.0 M", 14 Nov, was kept and read as his cell count over the 3 M he typed after it). |
| NOTE-2 | in force | **Every route's AI steps read them, as guidance.** Every attempt's workspace gets `user_notes.txt`; the model-led builder, the snappy planner and the FEA judgement calls are shown them and may use them only through the settings they already control, inside those settings' limits; the intake sets a note that fits a declared field (detail level, an engine parameter, a named boundary, far-field margins) there. A route with no AI step in its build (snapgrid's ECXML driver) gets them through the intake. `tests/unit/hygiene/test_user_notes_every_route.py` fails when a route or an engine has no reader. |
| NOTE-3 | in force | **A note is never a check.** The reviewer reads them as context, outside the text a finding may quote to ask for a rebuild, and never fails a mesh over one. What a note asks that cannot be done (removing holes from the CAD, say) is left and the run carries on. A step may leave one plain line on what it did with them, shown on the result as "From your notes: ...", best effort and never parsed or checked. |
| NOTE-4 | in force | **The notes' phrases about particular parts are tied to the parts by LOOKING at them** (`cad/part_words.py`, `render/part_pictures.py`; any route). CAD part names are often missing or meaningless, so the vision model (the reviewer route, the model the geometry check asks; never DeepSeek) is shown a table of the parts as measured - CAD name, box (longest first), volume, how full its box is, walls, bore and shaft diameters, how many alike, what each touches - and pictures: the whole model from three sides, every part its own colour with its number on it, and each part alone. It answers per phrase: the part ids, what is asked (a size, finer, coarser, a name), the user's own numbers and unit, the quote, why, and how sure. The rules check it: the quote is found word for word in the notes, a size's numbers are in the quote, min <= max and both positive, mm / cm / m / in converted to mm, every id a part, the size no finer than 1/2000 of the part and no coarser than the part itself, sure enough (0.6: "unsure" applies nothing), no part given two sizes. What fails applies nothing for that phrase, with a plain note; it never fails a run. The answer is kept and replayed by the decision cache like every AI step (AI-fea-6). Measured (5 Nov, lab, gpt-5.6-terra): a customer's 9-part bonded assembly and the note above - mapped right with the CAD names and again with every name blanked (by shape alone: the long hollow beam, the hex-headed cylinders, the flat rings, the short tubes, the plates with holes). **Whole part or a portion of it** (round 12, 6c): geometry decides first - an area the words resolve to that is the whole of its part (`cad/part_features.whole_of_part`: every other face of the part is an opening or an edge, a face under 4% of its area) IS the part, its row judged on the whole part's elements and met when they are the size asked; an area with faces of its own is that place. Where the geometry cannot tell (the words were tied to a part's id, which is both the part and every piece of it), the parts reader says which in its answer (`extent`: whole, portion or unsure, with `extent_words` quoted from the user's words and checked): only "whole" with the words found in the quote is the whole part; a portion, unsure, a missing extent or words not the user's are told as the nearest there is. No word lists: the name a user gives a part ("the base plate" of a one-plate motor mount, "the mount", "the body", "the part", in any language) is the part; "the vertical leg" of an L plate that is one face is a portion. The one-plate rule (NOTE-6) only removes the "which side" question. Cases: `devtools/validation/part_words_ab_extent.json`. **Round 13:** on a model of ONE body, which part the words mean is never in doubt - a reading below the sureness bar on that part is unsure only of the extent, so it is a portion told as the nearest there is, never "not clear which parts you meant" (6b: 0.45 on the L plate's leg dropped the place from the shell); and a check answered from an earlier session's check of the same bytes (`application/geometry_reuse`) is reused only when its areas were read by the rules in force (`cad/part_features.VERSION` in the reuse key; 6c's table came from a check whose areas had no level / upright split). |
| NOTE-5 | in force | **The user sees which parts their notes mean BEFORE they approve, and fixes it in one click** (`application/part_matches.py`). The geometry check keeps a model of two or more parts as it measured it - NOTE-4's facts table and pictures, on the labelled surface that exists only there - beside the check. When the request card is drawn, the notes and the parts are hashed; a reading of that key is shown, else one is queued on the geometry worker (the model can take 3 minutes; never on a request) and the card says "Reading your notes…" until it lands. The card shows one picture - each row's parts in the row's colour, the rest grey; hovering or opening a row shows only its parts; a part's number only where that part is SEEN (read off an id picture, no two numbers touching; the model's own pictures too); a second side offered only when the first hides a part - and a row per phrase - the user's words, the parts, the size ("frame rail · part 9 · 3–4 mm") - and a line per phrase the model was not sure of ("Couldn't tell which part is …"); nothing when the notes name no part, or when the AI step did not answer (the run then reads the notes itself). A row opens the part list; a click on a part - in the list or on the picture - keeps the pick at once, checked by NOTE-4's rules as the user's own (sure; the quote and the numbers still the notes', the size still sane for each part); no part picked puts the phrase back to "which parts?". There is no extra button or confirm step: the normal Approve takes the reading as the user left it, and the route uses it instead of asking again - only for the notes as they stand (a reading of older notes is never used), and only on parts the run has (same id, same name where both have one). |
| NOTE-6 | in force | **Areas inside a part are named and matched like parts** (`cad/part_features.py`). From the FEA measurement's own faces and hot spots: each hole or group of alike holes, each bore, notch, group of alike fillets, boss or shaft group, the thin walls (ribs, flanges: under half the part's typical wall) and the largest flat faces - each face in one area at most, 40 at most. | NOTE-7 | in force | **What the notes ask of the WHOLE mesh is read into one closed, typed set and applied** (`engines/mesh_requirements.py`; Areen, 10 Nov: "the notes are the place where the person can specify whatever requirements they want for their mesh; if they describe anything there, the system changes the parameters to what they describe"). It reads ONLY the user's own words - what they typed in the notes box and their own chat messages as kept (`contracts/user_notes.own_notes`) - never the intake's summary of the request, the geometry declaration or any text the application wrote (a user turn beginning with the geometry check's marks is never kept as a note), so a "Not applied" line can only quote the user. The builder model answers one strict call from those words: sizes (a global size, the shortest and the longest element edge, growth, a wall thickness), the element kind (quads only, triangles only, a most share of triangles, linear, quadratic) and quality limits, each a measure from a declared list (aspect ratio, warpage, Jacobian, skew, taper, the smallest and largest corner angle, non-orthogonality, skewness), an element shape, a direction, a target, the share of elements allowed past it and the outer limit none may pass - every entry with the user's own quote. The rules check each entry: the quote is found in the notes (a quote off by a character is read as the user's own line, and checked against that line); every number is in the quote (a fraction is its value, "for 99% of elements" allows 1%); the unit is the user's - the one written beside the number, else the one the notes give once for all, else millimetres, and said (mm, cm, m, inch, micron, or a multiple of the global size); a direction is the measure's own (an aspect ratio "over 5" is refused); an outer limit lies past its target; sizes and limits are in sane ranges; of two entries for one thing the LATEST written is kept, by where the user wrote it (Areen, 14 Nov: the user changed their mind; the earlier one is not told back as a fault - two that disagree inside ONE note, a floor over a ceiling, are still a contradiction, told); a number the user names as the one being replaced ("3 prism layers instead of 8", "not 8", "rather than 8", "from 8 to 3") is no request - only the value asked for is read, and a reading of the replaced number alone is told; a shortest edge over the longest, or over the global size, is not applied. What fails is dropped and told in plain words. The reading is cited, said before any mesh is made (one short line each: "From your notes: global element size 10 mm."), kept in the plan's words, and replayed by the AI decision cache. Per-part sizes (NOTE-4, AI-fea-9), an element count (AI-fea-4) and shell or solid (SHSTAGE) stay with their own readers. The shell mesh applies the set (`engines/shell_requirements.py`: its sizes, the limits its tidy-up steps aim at, the element kind), the solid mesh its sizes (PLAN-fea-33), a mixed mesh both; every route says what was asked and what was delivered (PLAN-fea-34, and the shell's report), and a requirement not reached is a note on a delivered mesh - never a failed run, and never told as met. On the flow route every CFD engine applies it (NOTE-10, CFDREQ-1..7). |

The team standard's own words keep STD-1's lines. **Every typed ask has its row** (m485w round 15): the report's "You asked" list - solid, shell and flow, in the result and in mesh_report.html - carries every ask the reading holds, met or not; one with nothing to judge it by is told with its status and figure in plain words (`mesh_requirements.told_status`: an edge-length target past its share "36.6% of elements under | Target, not met", "Applied (a setting)", "Nearest there is", "Not applied"), never judged (it never makes a result "not fully checked") and never one of the verdict's limits. | NOTE-13 | in force | **A control is declared once** (rulebook 73, `contracts/mesh_controls.py`, `engines/control_appliers.py`). Every control a user's words may set is one entry of `CONTROLS`: its key, kind (number, length, count, choice, flag), unit, sane range, where it may apply (the whole mesh, or a feature kind), the line the notes' reader is shown and example phrasings, routes, stage (geometry, mesh, delivery), its rule, how it is verified ("setting": applied as a setting, never told as met), and what EVERY mesher lane does with it - an applier, the older code that already reads it (`Legacy`), or `NotHere(why)`. From that one table: the reader's ONE generic `controls` output and its instructions (NOTE-7's checks: the quote is the user's, the number is in it and not the one being replaced, the unit is theirs, the range is sane, a choice is named in their words, a whole-mesh control is not taken for one place, the latest wins); one loop at each plan seam (`control_appliers.apply`: the FEA solid's make_plan, snappyHexMesh's internal driver through `Ask.controls`) that applies each control or says "not applied - <why>"; and one row in each route's report (`control_appliers.report_row`, the solid's and the shell's; on the flow route every control row an engine did not decide says the registry's reason). `tests/unit/hygiene/test_mesh_controls_declared.py` fails when a control does not say what every lane does with it. The controls today: the growth rate (the reader's own field; on a solid the plan's growth, PLAN-fea-6's 1.3 - a gentler grading the ladder needed to build valid elements, LAD-fea-1, is kept and said); elements through the thickness of every wall on a solid (each wall zone sized thickness / N; a hot spot's wall keeps its own 3 when that is more; applied AFTER a size range the user set - in the notes or in the saved meshing standard, PLAN-fea-29/33 - so the count, the more exact ask, wins over the range's shortest edge, and the plan says where it made the walls finer than that edge; the FEA AI is told the count is the rules', so its remark never says it is not applied; the plans the stage made before the notes were written are never reused when the notes set a control (PLAN-fea-22's reuse skipped: job 5656805e reused one and meshed 2 through for "5 elements through the thickness"); and its row is asked against delivered - the fewest elements measured through a planned wall, read to within 10%, met or not met, never only "applied as a setting"; the verdict's wall row, the result's wall line and the report's header hold the mesh to the count asked ("Elements through the thinnest wall (5 planned, as your notes ask; read to within 10%)", limit 4.5; "every wall at least 5 elements thick"), our 2 only when none was asked; the notes are read before the FEA AI step, which is shown the asks the rules apply and report themselves (`fea_ai.rules_asks`: every row but an element count, which it reads, and a size in one named place) and leaves them out of its remark, and the parts reader's words are a line of the result only when it tied, set aside or was unsure of a phrase, or the notes ask a size in one named place); cells across a passage and the wall's refinement level on snappyHexMesh's internal flow (at least 8 across, CFDREQ-2's validity floor); the solver files' length unit (read and told: every file is written in metres, millimetres are not offered yet). Cells across a passage reach VMTK (its edge factor is 2 / N; the budget's fit may coarsen it, said with the count) and cfMesh (the wall band's cell is the passage's width / N; the cells the octree builds are said) through the same loop. **Where an ask applies**: each control declares its own subject words in the registry (`Control.subject`: the walls and their thickness; the pipe, bore, passage, duct, inlet; the walls, surface, body); a `where` made only of them and words meaning "all of it" ("each wall", "the wall thickness everywhere", "the inlet passage", "the bore") is the whole-mesh ask (`mesh_controls.place_of`), and the reader is told to leave `where` empty then. A `where` naming one place among several ("the left flange") is applied to the whole mesh - the nearest there is - and said on its row ("... (asked for the left flange: set on the whole mesh, the nearest there is)"; "... - on the whole mesh, not only the left flange: a setting for one place is not offered yet"), never dropped to our default (jobs bfdc60b1, f2c6fc7d: 2 through for 4 and 5; every cells-across ask). **The rules decide, the AI steps do not repeat it**: every ask the reading holds - a row, an ask the checks set aside, words no setting matches (`mesh_requirements.decided_asks`) - is shown to the FEA AI step, the snappy planner and the CFD builder model as decided by the rules, to plan as if not asked and to leave out of their remark (an element count stays the FEA AI's to read). A growth written as a percent is its ratio, in the reader's words and in the check ("no more than 25% from one to the next" is 1.25). The notes block's own labels ("[n1] (typed in the notes box)") are never told back as the user's words; an answer to one of the route's own questions (what the mesh is for, CFD or FEA, the analysis, through or around) with no number in it is no note (`user_notes.capture`'s `route_question`), and the solid AI's remark is never a line of a delivered shell's result. On a solid, a size in one named place the parts reader tied to nothing is "not applied - no such place was found on the part (the fillets)", never "applied as a setting"; the parts reader is shown the plan's rounded edges as fillets (`cad/part_features`: a curved face with a radius that is no plane, cylinder or cone - a torus, a B-spline round, the plan's own PLAN-fea-7 rounds (fea0254: 16 B-spline faces of radius 0.141 mm) - or a hollow cylinder that is no hole or bore; a convex cylinder stays a boss or shaft). | NOTE-14 | in force | **How strict a limit is, in the user's own words: hard, flexible, or unstated** (rulebook 73, `engines/mesh_requirements.py` `strictness`, `apply_strictness`, `STRICT_DEFAULT`; `engines/meshing_standard.merge`). THE READING: the notes reader returns a `strictness` list - each entry a target (one limit: global, min_edge, max_edge, near_wall, a quality measure, count; or a group: minimums, maximums, sizes, quality, angles, counts, all), "hard" or "flexible", and the quote. The model decides it from the words ("we're flexible around the minimum of 5mm, doesn't need to be a hard min", "the angle limits are hard", "these are guidelines"); the rules check it as every field: the quote is the user's, and a quote with a number names only the rows whose own words hold that number. The entries apply in the order written, the saved standard's first and the notes' after, so a later sentence refines an earlier one; one that names no limit set is told not applied. Each requirement row it names carries `strictness` ("hard" / "flexible") and `strictness_quote`; a row the user said nothing about carries neither. THE MEANING: **flexible** is a target - the mesher may go outside it where that keeps what the analyst wants more (rings round bolt holes, a regular grid, clean rows, fewer triangles, the part's shape), and the result never says "not met" for it: the row says how many elements are outside and by how much ("flexible - 1.5% of elements (n) are shorter, the shortest 3 mm"), the tally counts it as a flexible limit, and the 'You asked' row is information. **hard** is a gate - the mesher gives structure up before it breaks it, and if it cannot hold it, the nearest and told, as before; a hard edge length is judged (met only with no element outside). **Unstated** (`STRICT_DEFAULT`, `strictness(row)`): an edge length (global, min_edge, max_edge, near_wall) is flexible - criteria lists state lengths as targets and the meshes made to them break them in places (a reference mesh sent to us had 3.3% of its elements under its own stated minimum), and every route already reports them with the share outside, never as missed; a quality limit is held as written - a stated allowance ("1% between 15° and 30°") is that limit's own flexibility, hard at the outer value and flexible inside the allowance; a limit with no allowance, and a count, are hard. So a run where nobody says how strict a limit is is reported and meshed exactly as before, and a run with no notes reads nothing. CONSUMERS read `strictness(row)` (the user's word, else the default): the solid FEA plan lets the zones the accuracy rules make finer keep their size under a flexible shortest edge (`fea_requirements.whole_sizes` "min_flexible", `fea_plan.user_sized`). A size for the whole mesh that came out far finer than asked (under half: the part is smaller than the size, or its walls, holes and curves need smaller elements) says the same true thing in the row and in the plan - asked, built and why ("elements are about 0.95 mm, finer than the 10 mm asked: your part is 13 mm across, so its walls, holes and curves need smaller elements to keep their shape", `finer_global`, `fea_plan.user_words`); flexible (the default for a size) it is information, hard it is told not met, and a shell never says "coarser than our accuracy rules" over elements built finer than asked. On a flow mesh a hard smallest cell is held by cfMesh, VMTK and the tet routes as they already hold it; on snappyHexMesh the level that closes the body off from the flow comes before it (validity), told so. **Told on every route, never by a model** (round 12, m484x A3, A6): a shell's plan says how strict its limits are, as a solid's does ("aspect ratio under 4 - flexible - a target: ..."); a note on how strict the limits are that names no limit the user set gets one fixed line, before meshing and on the result ("From your notes: your note set no limit, so nothing in the mesh changed because of it.") - never "not applied", never counted. A strictness is one of the asks the rules decide (`mesh_requirements.decided_asks`): the reading is kept in the workspace, and no AI step's notes_remark joins the result beside it - the FEA AI step's included (`ai_remark_kept_for`; A6 read "Treated the flexible note as guidance; all geometry remains present ..."). |
| SHMESH-52 | in force | **A size the notes ask in ONE named place is applied on a shell mesh** (rulebook 73, `engines/shell_places.py`, `engines/gmsh/shell_adapter.py`; the control `place_size` of NOTE-13, the shell lane its applier; docs/design/shell/README.md for the other SHMESH rules). Before, a shell told "5 mm elements on the base plate" or "finer on the bracket" as not applied: the parts were read only for the solid plan, after the shell was delivered. Now, when the notes ask a size in one place (NOTE-10's `local`) and the mesh is a shell, the reading of which parts and areas the words mean (AI-fea-9 on NOTE-4 and NOTE-6) is made BEFORE the shell, once - the solid plan reuses it - and handed to the shell run. Only a reading whose quote is the quote of a `local` row is applied, so what is meshed is what is told. A part is every shell face of that body; an area is every shell face made from one of its CAD faces, and it wins over its part there (PLAN-fea-32). Each such face takes the size asked (the middle of a band), or its own size halved for "finer" or doubled for "coarser" (PLAN-fea-36's one step); a part with a number keeps it over a "finer" or "coarser". A curved face keeps its shape (SHMESH-24: 4 a turn, its middle a tenth of the size off the arc), a curved face asked coarser keeps its own size, and the faces beside are graded from it (SHMESH-6). The size asked wins over our own arc and hole sizes there, coarser or finer: a hole or arc of a face in the place is sized as under a size for the whole mesh (SHMESH-24), the size asked but never under SR.HOLE_PER_TURN (8) round a hole, so a washer ring keeps what it needs (Val4's flanges, 18 holes 4 mm across, asked 3 mm over our 2.5 mm: our 12 a turn round each hole graded the whole flange to about 1.3 mm; now about 2.1 mm, the rest held by the holes' 8 a turn and the finer bends beside). The user's size beats our minimums - finer than half the wall, coarser than our rules - and is said; only a plan too big to build is stepped to the nearest that builds, said. The report's row says asked against delivered on the elements there ("Elements of 2.5 mm at the top plate: met - top plate: elements are about 2.5 mm (812 elements).", met when inside the size or band read as about it, SHMESH-24's band). Its warnings come from the elements built there: a place that came out under 0.8 of its size says it is smaller near holes, bends and finer faces beside it; "coarser than our accuracy rules" is said only when its largest elements (the 90th percentile) are over the size our rules gave those faces, with both numbers. Words that mean only a piece of a part that is no face of its own ("the vertical leg" of an L plate that is one flat face: the parts reader says so, NOTE-4's piece flag) are set on the whole part and told "applied to the whole part, the nearest there is", never plainly met; what cannot be placed is told not applied with why: a hole, bore or notch is only an edge on a shell (the shell is the middle of the plate), a solid boss and a part that stays solid are not in it, a reading that was off, did not answer, was unsure or dropped the words says so. Nothing asked in one place: nothing is read before the shell and the mesh is byte for byte the one before. The shell part of a mixed mesh and a surface file still say it is not applied yet. A size set on the whole part as the nearest there is keeps its 'You asked' row in the report, with its own words (round 9: the L plate's leg is part of ONE flat face, so no face is it). |
| SHMESH-52b | in force | **A size asked on a face the shell makes an edge of goes on that edge** (rulebook 76, `engines/shell_places.py`, `engines/gmsh/shell_adapter._edge_place_curves`). A hole's bore, a notch or the plate's edge wall - a face picked on the model (NOTE-17) or a named hole (NOTE-6) - meets no mid-face: the shell is the middle of the plate, so that face is only an edge of it. Before, such a size was told "not applied". Now the face's own triangles are kept with the reading, and on the mesher's model every curve whose points all lie within a quarter of the wall of that face is sized to the size asked (the middle of a band), graded out as an arc's size is (SHMESH-6): the elements round the hole are about the size asked. A curve that only runs up to the face is not one. A size in mm only (an edge has no size of its own to halve or double). The report measures the elements touching that edge. A face that meets no mid-face and no curve is told not applied, as before; never guessed. |
| SHMESH-52c | in force | **"Finer" or "coarser" with no number on a face the shell makes an edge of is applied on that edge** (rulebook 86, `engines/shell_places.edge_scaled`, `engines/gmsh/shell_adapter._edge_place_curves` / `edge_own`). A hole's wall, a notch, a named hole or group of holes (NOTE-6) or a hole wall picked on the model (NOTE-17), asked "finer" ("refine") or "coarser" with no number: before, SHMESH-52b took a size in mm only, so the row said "not applied - a hole is only an edge on a shell" while the conversation had said it was made finer. Now each mid-surface curve lying on that face (SHMESH-52b's search) takes the size the mesh would have had along it - the plan's size round a hole of the faces it bounds (the washer and arc size, SHMESH-3/39), else those faces' own size - halved for "finer" or doubled for "coarser": PLAN-fea-36's one step, the same factor as on a shell face (SHMESH-52) and on a solid. It is graded out as an arc's size is (SHMESH-6); the hole is meshed free (no washer ring, as SHMESH-52b), and the mesh's floor is lowered to half of it when needed. "Coarser" is held to what the shell allows: never coarser than the faces it bounds (their own size meets the edge) and never under SR.HOLE_PER_TURN (8) elements round a hole; our finer arc size there is not set on it, and the curve is given its count (its length over that size), since gmsh's own curvature sizing (16 a turn) would otherwise keep a small hole as fine as before (public front panel: the 5 mm holes asked coarser stayed at 0.98 mm; with the count about 1.57 mm). A coarser that would change nothing anywhere is told not applied, with why. The report's row measures the elements touching that edge and the plate round it (the elements of those faces not on it): "a hole, 22 mm across: on a shell it is an edge; elements along it are about X mm (n elements), the plate round it about Y mm; the size it would have had there, B mm, halved to S mm". It is met only when clearly finer: at most 0.75 of both the plate round it and the size it would have had (0.75 = the solid's band for a halved size, 1.5 x 0.5); coarser, at least that size over 0.75. Otherwise it says it did not come out clearly finer (coarser). A size in mm on an edge and "finer" on a face of the mid-surface are unchanged. |
| SHMESH-11h | in force | **An open offset face of a large skin is not re-made before the seam cut** (`engines/gmsh/shell_adapter.py`: `_offset_worth`, `OFFSET_OPEN_POLES` = 2500; `split_closed`): SHMESH-11g re-makes an offset face as a B-spline before it is cut at its seams; now only a face with a seam edge or on a closed or periodic surface, or an open one whose base surface has at most 2500 poles. Neither can pass the run's HARD CAP, told to the mesh beside its usual stop (SHELL_CAP_ENV): the solid route's shell run at most half of what the attempt has left (the solid mesh delivered when no shell is made keeps the other half) and the machine's wall (13,800 s on Cloud Run); the surface run at most 0.8 of it and the same wall. A price the cap cuts is said: the record's clock `capped`, and the user's line says it had all the time this job had left. The surface run now tells the shell mesh its stops too (before: the mesh kept its own 900 s and the run could be cut from outside with no mesh). A stamped part's fillets, narrow flanks and run-out slivers are faces narrower than the shortest edge the user allows: meshed as faces, every element on them had an edge across the strip under that edge (on 20 synthetic stamped parts at 3 to 8 mm, 48% of all elements). | NOTE-15 | in force | **What the product acted on is never told as not applied** (rulebook 73, `engines/notes_acted.py`). Some of what the notes say is acted on outside the reading, and the reading cannot know it. Before a "Not applied ... no mesh setting of ours matches it yet" line is shown or counted, the sentence is checked against what the product did: **the engine the notes name** - one sentence that names ONE engine and plainly chooses it, by the typed override's own rule (`engine_selection.engine_chosen_in`; "mesh it with VMTK" too; never a question, a refusal or "VMTK failed last time"), the latest one winning - is an ask of the report whatever the reading made of it (not applied, no mesh setting, set aside): "From your notes: meshed with snappyHexMesh, as you asked.", a row met when the engine that built the mesh is the one named; one that could not be used says why and what made the mesh ("you asked for cfMesh; cfMesh did not make a usable mesh, so this mesh was made with snappyHexMesh"); **the flow conditions** the user confirmed at intake ("water at 2 m/s": taken as the flow conditions you confirmed); **the kind of mesh**, when it is the kind this run makes ("internal flow mesh"; "external" on an internal run stays not applied); **the unit of the notes' own numbers** ("Units: mm"). A sentence that is only one of these (its own words, and words a row of the reading already holds) is taken out of the lists and told by the act's line; one that asks anything more stays not applied. The same correction is made on the request card, on the flow route (once per attempt, with that attempt's engine) and on the solid route. m483 round 12, 5a: "use snappyHexMesh" meshed with snappyHexMesh and was told "Not applied" and counted as "1 other thing ... could not be applied". **The wall treatment in words is the y+ ask** (m485w L5-L7): "resolve the wall", "wall-resolved", "a resolved boundary layer", "no wall functions", "low-Re" (a resolved wall) and "wall functions" (CFDREQ-8's two treatments) are the same ask as the y+ that builds them - with a y+ row (at or under 5: resolved; at or over 20: wall functions) or the treatment confirmed at intake they are one ask, met together; alone, "resolve the wall" is the ask itself (y+ about 1 at the walls, sized from the confirmed fluid and speed); words naming the other treatment than the one built stay told. And the general guard: words whose every word an applied row already holds, naming what that row asks, are that row's - never told again as not applied; a true extra ask beside them still is. |
| NOTE-16 | in force | **Pictures and files the user attaches are read like their typed words** (`application/chat_attachments.py`, `contracts/user_notes.py`, `contracts/user_pictures.py`). In the chat box and the notes box the user can paste a screenshot, drop or attach a picture (PNG, JPG, WebP, up to 10 MB) or a file that holds text (PDF, TXT, MD, CSV, up to 20 MB), at most 5 to one message or note. Every file is checked by its bytes, never its name; every picture is re-encoded with its metadata stripped and its pixels capped; a PDF's text is read with page, character and time caps. The files are kept per session and tenant and served only to their owner, never executable. A message or note keeps the attached files' rows (never their bytes) and a message that carries one is always kept as a note. The text of an attached file counts as the user's own words (NOTE-7's quote rule); a number seen only in a picture is not applied - the reply asks the user to type it. Pictures reach the chat model only when its route declares vision, else the chat says in one line it could not look at the picture; they always reach the parts reader (NOTE-4): the newest 4, each beside the note it came with, the reader told they show WHERE the words mean and to say so when it cannot tell. |
