ONE RULE for the run and the parts checklist (`engines/mesh_requirements.picked_for`, called by `bind_picks` and `application/part_matches.with_faces`, rulebook 76): the words of a note sent with picks are for those faces, whatever place they name ("here", "the bore") - the checklist row shows the face chip (`picked: true`), never the part reader's place - and a global size typed with a pick becomes a size on those faces, never the global size; only words that say the whole mesh are left as written; the place is told as the user saw it ("the face you picked (Face 12 · round Ø15 mm)"). Each route is to apply it on those faces (the entry's `faces`) or tell why not. FEA solid and shell: the picked faces are an area of their own, `Pick_n` (`cad/part_words.with_picks`), each face kept only when it is still the face picked on the run's own surface (`cad/face_map.same_face`: area and centre), else told "no longer on the model" and no other face guessed; the solid report measures the elements on those faces. snappyHexMesh: a finer size on picked faces is a distance refinement on their own triangles (`engines/snappy/part_levels.picked_surfaces`, the check's face map read through `contracts/geometry_check.face_map`, re-checked by `same_face`); coarser or layers on one face are told with the next best. On a model of one body, a size in a place that is not located is told, never put on every wall. |
| NOTE-18 | in force | **The plan step is a conversation; saying go starts the mesh** (rulebook 82, `agents/intake/plan_talk.py`, called from `agents/intake/message._settle` while the plan's approval is live; the reply in `api/v1/chat._requirement_reply`). Once the plan card stands, the chat box is where mesh requirements are typed (there is no separate notes box). Each message is read (rulebook 85, `agents/intake/plan_go.py`): CODE decides the safe cases with no model - a message that carries a face, a picture or a file, or whose words hold a number, a unit, a size or a mesh word (finer, size, layers, quality...), is a requirement and never a go, so "go finer on the bore" is a requirement; the Proceed button's own text is a go; a plan word (engine, flow kind, unit) is the intake's. THE REST (short words with no requirement in them) is asked of the intake's own model, one small question with a strict answer set - go / wait / question / requirement / other - given our last line and the message, within 3 s. **go** starts the run (exactly as the Proceed button does) only when the model says go AND code found no requirement content, no question mark, no negation or condition ("not", "don't", "but", "first") - a no that only says nothing more is wanted and then a clear go is no negation ("nope, nothing else. go", "no, that's all, mesh it": every clause before the last a no to more or a nothing-more, the last a go that acts; never "no go", "don't go yet", "nothing else to go on"; `plan_talk._done_then_go`, rulebook 91) and no plain "ok" to another question of ours; the model's "wait" is a hold, its "question" goes to the intake, anything else is asked "Start the mesh now?" once. The model failing or slower than 3 s: the phrase reading decides (every clause a go clause or filler - "ok I'm good to mesh", "no more changes", "that's all from me, mesh it" - or the intake's consent words), and where it is unsure the go question is asked once; a go-like sentence is never answered "couldn't read that as a mesh requirement". **ask** - a go asked back ("ready to mesh?", "can we proceed?") or a hedge ("maybe"): "Start the mesh now?" is asked once; asked already, the message is kept as a requirement. **hold** - "wait", "not yet", "don't proceed yet", "hold on": nothing starts and nothing is kept, and the reply says so ("OK, I won't start yet. Tell me when you're ready, or add anything else you want first."). **question** - a question about the mesh itself, in question words with or without a question mark and whatever the typos ("what will the cell count be?", "so for this mesh basedon my rquirements, what would the elemwnt size be roughly"): nothing is kept and it is answered from the plan's own numbers (NOTE-20, rulebook 88); the model's "question" about the mesh's count, time or settings is that too, and its "requirement" whose words ask a question is answered as a question. **intake** - any other question, or a change to the plan itself (the engine, internal or external, the domain, the unit): the intake answers or re-plans, as before. A requirement that also names another engine than the plan's ("use cfMesh at 2 mm") is both (rulebook 84): kept as a requirement AND handed to the intake, which switches the plan. **requirement** - everything else, and always a message that carries pictures, files or picked faces: kept as the user's note of that turn (exactly as typed, with its files and faces: NOTE-1, NOTE-16, NOTE-17), the plan stands one turn further on, and the checklist reading NOTE-5's Finalize notes started is started at once for the notes as they stand; the reply says what the checklist took from THIS message (its rows, found by the note id the reading stamps on them, rulebook 84), written by the model under NOTE-19's guard, the plain first-person sentence below its fallback (rulebook 85): "I've set 1 mm elements on Face 32 (round Ø6 mm).", "I've set 1 mm elements on the 4 faces you picked.", a place the reader chose "I've made the mesh finer at the fillet (radius 8 mm). That's my guess from your words; click it on the model if you meant another spot.", a phrase it could not place, the flow reader's own lines on a flow mesh; nothing taken: the words are still kept, and the reply says so and what it can take (NOTE-25; before rulebook 94 "I couldn't find a mesh setting in that.") - only for words that state something to set: words that ask a question are answered as one (NOTE-20); a picture: kept with the note and shown, but not read itself, so the place comes from the words or a clicked face (said once, only when a picture came); a message with picked faces and one plain size in mm and nothing else is answered at once from its known row, without waiting for the reading (rulebook 85); a model with no checklist before the run: kept, read when the mesh is built. |
| NOTE-19 | in force | **A requirement's reply is written by the model, under a guard** (rulebook 84, `agents/intake/plan_reply.py`). The answer to a requirement at the plan step (NOTE-18) is written by the intake's own model (`call_intake_model`: its client, model and settings; no other provider) answering THE PERSON as a friendly colleague does in chat (rulebook 85): first person ("I've set", "I'll go finer", "I took that as"), contractions, a short acknowledgement that fits what they said ("Sure" to a "can you...?", "Got it" to a statement), then what is set and where in its own words; one or two short sentences, varied (asked of the prompt, with example exchanges in that voice), no filler, no emoji, no exclamation marks, in the user's language. It is given the message (its words, that a picture or file came with it, the faces picked) and THE FACTS the checklist produced for it - what will be applied, where (face labels, features), whether the place was the user's click or the model's own guess (said as its guess, and that a click on the model corrects it), that a picture is kept but not read, what could not be placed - with the plain sentence that says exactly that. Its prompt is composed only from its own block registry (`PLAN_REPLY_PROMPT_BLOCKS`). A GUARD in code (`plan_reply.check`) refuses a reply holding a number the facts do not hold, naming no place when something was applied (a face id, the picked faces by their count or as "those faces", a feature's noun or size, or "the whole mesh" each name it), giving a guessed place as certain, claiming something was applied when nothing was, citing a rule id, longer than 300 characters, or exclaiming (no longer: opening like our last reply, rulebook 85); which check refused it is logged; refused, failed or slower than 6 s, the plain sentence (`plan_talk.reply_for`) is said. Which one was said is logged, never shown. A row is this message's by the id of the note the reading read it from (`application/part_matches.with_notes`, by the reader's own word-for-word test), never by matching its words again. No request is held for the reading: the send waits at most 12 s for it, then answers with `reply_later` and the reply comes with the card's poll of the reading (GET `.../check/parts`, its `reply`), written once and kept in the conversation; a reading that failed says so and keeps the note. The list of what will be applied and the one Proceed stand in a slim bar pinned above the chat box at the plan step (a count that opens the list), not in the card. |

A message that asks ABOUT the mesh - its size, element count, how fine, how long, what was set - in question words (a question word anywhere with a word after it, never "how about" / "what if"; a lead-in such as "tell me"; a clause opening on an auxiliary not addressed to us, never "will you..."), with or without a question mark and whatever the typos, and with no number in it, is a question in code before any model is asked (a question about the KIND of mesh - element type, order, engine, or a why - which no number answers, stays the intake's, as before); the plan-step model's "question" about the mesh's count, time or settings is one too, and its "requirement" whose words ask a question is answered as a question.
