Click the face you mean and send that again." It waits for a click the way NOTE-21's ask does: a click sent alone next applies the words to exactly the faces clicked (NOTE-22). What counts as having the kind: an area of it in the table (holes and bores are one; a flange or rib is a thin wall or a large flat face; a bend a rounded edge, a thin wall or a large flat face); a part that is one plate is its own thin wall. Read as before: words with no place ("make it finer" is the whole part), words that name any kind the part has (plural = all of them), words placed by a pick, a model of more than one body (a part's own name may be the word, NOTE-4/5), a part whose name holds the word, a model whose faces were not measured (the flow route's staged STL; its role words, CFDREQ-12), and an area list cut at its most (40). |
| NOTE-24 | in force | **An element or cell count typed at the plan step is a row of the checklist, and the reply says it** (rulebook 93, `agents/intake/plan_talk.count_asks`, `plan_talk.count_rows`, `application/part_matches.with_counts`, `agents/intake/plan_reply.facts`, `plan_reply._count_check`). Before, the plan-step checklist on an FEA mesh came only from the parts reader (NOTE-5), which reads sizes in places and never a count, so "keep it under 1.5 million elements" got "I couldn't find a mesh setting in that" (deployed 91926483, FEA solid). Now a count the words state for the whole mesh - a number with its multiplier ("1.5 million", "800k", "500,000", "2M") before "elements" / "cells" / "tets" / "hexes", or after "element count", at least 1,000 - is read in code, with its bound: under / at most / max / up to / no more than (and "max" or "or fewer" after it) is a ceiling, at least / over / min (and "or more") a floor, anything else about. A range ("700k-1.5M", "between 1 and 2 million") and a number being replaced ("instead of 8 million") are left to the run's reader; a size ("3 mm elements"), a count under 1,000 ("2 elements through the wall") and a question with no number (NOTE-20) are no count. The newest note that states one gives the row: what (an element count, the whole mesh), the bound and the number as typed ("at most 1.5 million elements"; "cells" on a flow mesh), stamped with that note's id. It is shown only where the run's own reader applies it: every flow mesh (CFDREQ-3), and the solid and shell meshes while their AI step reads the notes (AI-fea-4 -> PLAN-fea-27 / SHMESH-23; with that step off the run applies no count and no row is shown). The reply is written by the model under NOTE-19's guard, from facts that hold the count (its bound and number as typed, no place needed), what is already on the checklist, and - when sizes the user typed are on the checklist too - which one wins on this route: on the solid and shell meshes the sizes typed are built and the count moves only the rest (PLAN-fea-29, PLAN-fea-33, SHMESH-24); on a flow mesh the count comes first (CFDREQ-3); either way the result says what could not be held. The guard also refuses a reply that does not say the count's number as typed, says it with another bound, or - beside such sizes - promises it or gives no condition. Its fallback is said in code: "I'll keep it under 1.5 million elements." ("I'll make it at least 2 million cells.", "I'll aim for about 800k elements."), then, beside typed sizes, "If that can't hold with the sizes you typed, your sizes win and the result will say so." (flow: "... the count comes first and the result will say where the sizes gave way."). The plan step does not settle a count against a size; the run does, and tells it. |
| NOTE-25 | in force | **Words the checklist takes nothing from are kept, and the reply says so truthfully** (rulebook 94, `agents/intake/plan_talk.took_nothing`, `plan_talk.NOT_READ`, `plan_talk.kept_reply`, `plan_talk.geometry_edit`, `agents/intake/plan_reply._kept_facts`, `plan_reply.check`). A statement at the plan step is kept as the user's note before it is read (NOTE-18), every route's planning step is handed the notes as guidance (NOTE-2: `user_notes.txt`, the builder, the snappy planner, the FEA judgement calls, the requirements reader), and the result tells each ask back as applied, the nearest, not applied with the reason, or not a mesh setting (NOTE-8, NOTE-12). So a statement the checklist took nothing from ("make it look nice", "it's for a stress analysis") is no longer answered "I couldn't find a mesh setting in that" (Areen, 25 Nov: what the user types gets built; acknowledged and kept, never turned away). The reply reads like a person talking back (Areen, 25 Nov: "make it reply like an LLM is talking back to us"; the first wording, "That isn't something I can lock in on the checklist, but I've kept ... for mesh planning. ... You can set a size, refinement, quality limit, element count, or click a face.", read as a form and was dropped): written by the model under NOTE-19's guard, ONE short sentence, two at most, that says what they told it in its own words and that it will be kept in mind when the mesh is planned ("Got it, cast aluminium. I'll keep that in mind when the mesh is planned."). It adds that the result will say what came of it only when the words ask something of the mesh (`plan_talk.asks_of_the_mesh`: a requirement word or "mesh", as in "make it a good quality mesh please"), never for plain context such as a material. Words that ask for a change to the CAD itself - removing or filling holes, fillets, chamfers, slots and the like - are told that meshing cannot do that and the mesh follows the part as it is, that they are kept, and that the result will say it was not applied. The guard refuses a reply to either that claims anything was set, applied, done or guaranteed (unless negated: "it wasn't applied", or said as a condition: "the result will say if it was applied", "whether this mesher could apply it", "it may be applied"), that does not say the words were kept (in any natural words: "noted", "I'll keep that in mind", "I'll remember that for the planning"), or that says "mesh setting" - the same claim test answers a flow model's requirement kept for the run (the real model's "I've noted ... The result will show whether this mesher could apply it." was refused as a claim before); to words kept for the planning also one that says "checklist" or "lock in", lists what can be set (or asks for a click), runs past two sentences (three with a picture), quotes their sentence back in quotation marks, or speaks of the result for plain context. Its fallback, said in code (`plan_talk.kept_reply`): "Noted. I'll keep that in mind when the mesh is planned." (words that ask something of the mesh: "Noted. I'll keep that in mind when the mesh is planned, and the result will say what came of it."; the CAD change: "Removing or filling features changes the CAD itself, which meshing can't do, so the mesh follows the part as it is. I've kept your words, and the result will say that wasn't applied."). Unchanged: questions (NOTE-20), a hold, a go, the one-place asks and their lines (NOTE-21, NOTE-22, NOTE-23), and a flow model with no checklist before the run (kept, read when the mesh is built). |

## STD: the team's meshing standard (settings)

Areen, 11 Nov: a team meshes a standard way - a quality criteria list it always uses, a default element size, units,
a solver - and should not have to type it every run. The console's Meshing settings hold ONE standard per
organisation, written as the notes are written (`engines/meshing_standard.py`; storage `organization_meshing_standards`,
migration 0020; routes GET/PUT `/api/v1/organization/meshing-standard`). Settings are the team's standing
requirements; the notes are this run's; the notes win, field by field.

| ID | Status | Rule |
|---|---|---|
| STD-1 | in force | **One reader, read once.** The standard is read by NOTE-7's own prompt, tool and checks, shown to the model as a note of its own ("the team's meshing standard, from settings") with no route in the question, so the reading made when it is SAVED serves every run: the model's answer and the key of the question are kept with the words, and a run re-checks that answer by the rules with no model call. Changed words or a changed question (a new prompt or tool): the run reads it again through the builder route and the decision cache, with NOTE-9's fallbacks. A model down at save time still saves the words (each run reads them). The run is dispatched with the standard as it stood at approval (a dispute re-run keeps its parent's). A standard that names a solver file every FEA run delivers (Nastran, Abaqus, CalculiX, Ansys) is told as delivered, never "not applied"; its words for one part, an element count or shell or solid are told as not applied ("put ... in a run's notes"). |
| STD-2 | in force | **Rules < settings < notes, field by field** (`engines/meshing_standard.merge`, the one merge every route calls where it read the notes). A field is a size of one kind, one element-kind choice (quads or triangles or a share of triangles; the order), a quality limit per measure, element shape and direction, and on the flow route each of its own fields and each named place. What the run's notes set wins for that run; every other field of the standard stays. The notes' lengths with no unit take the unit the standard names; an edge length written as a multiple of the global size follows the global size in force; where a value of the standard contradicts one of the notes, the notes' is kept and the standard's told as not applied. Every row of the set says where it came from: said before meshing ("From your settings: ..." / "From your notes: ..."), on each asked-against-delivered row ("Your settings ask: ...", "(from your settings)"), and in the closing line ("From your settings and notes: ..."). A part the notes size keeps its size; the standard's global size covers the rest (PLAN-fea-33). On the flow route (NOTE-10, CFDREQ-1) the CFD reading step (`cfd_requirements.read_for_attempt`) makes the same call: every CFD engine's set is the merged one, the flow report's rows carry where each came from and its closing line is led the same way; the flow fields' own rules hold across the two sources (no boundary layers against a layer setting, a first layer not thinner than the stack, a cell floor not under the ceiling, a count outside the range: the notes' kept, the standard's told), and the standard's unit reaches the flow lengths (the size at the walls, a layer's first height and stack, a size in one named place). |
| STD-3 | in force | **Nothing in either, nothing changes.** With no standard the notes' own section is returned untouched: every plan, mesh and record is byte for byte the run before the standard existed (NOTE-9 then holds as before). |
| STD-4 | in force | **The settings switch and the stage on a shell's elements.** The Meshing settings page has one switch beside its box, "Shell elements: Quads | Triangles" (Quads by default; `organization_meshing_standards.shell_elements`, 0021), the settings' own value of the shell element kind: Triangles is triangles only; Quads sets aside a "triangles only" the standard's words gave (told) and keeps a "quads only" or a share of triangles (`engines/meshing_standard.switched`). Then STD-2 as before - the notes win - and last the stage (`engines/meshing_standard.stage`): a click on the stage's Quads | Triangles is the last word for that run, recorded with source "stage" (Triangles: triangles only; Quads: quad-dominant - only a "triangles only" gives way). One field, kind.shell; the result says which and where from: "Triangles, from your settings.", "Triangles, from your notes.", "Quads, chosen on the stage.", "Quads, the default." A switch on Quads with no words is no standard (STD-3). |
| STD-5 | in force | **A saved standard applies to the kinds of mesh it is written for** (rulebook 73, `engines/meshing_standard.merge`, `_kinds_of`, `_fits`; the reader's `applies_to`). When the standard is read, the AI tags the list as a whole and each line that differs from it with the kinds of mesh it speaks to - shell, solid, cfd, any - and the rules check each line's quote is the user's. A structural quality list (warpage, quad and tria angles, Jacobian, element length rules) is shell and solid, its global size too: on a flow mesh none of it applies, and the run is the run with no standard - every engine's dictionaries byte for byte - told once before meshing and with the result: "Your saved meshing standard is written for shell and solid meshes; it was not applied to this flow mesh." A list written for CFD is likewise not applied to a solid or shell mesh. A general line ("export in mm", "at most 2 million cells or elements") applies to all; a mixed list splits line by line, and the lines left out are named. The job's own notes always apply as typed. A standard read before the tag existed applies as it always did. (m483 with the saved list on: a 10-line structural list set CFD cell sizes - wall level 3 to 1 - and judged their aspect ratio.) |

## CFDREQ: the user's requirements on a flow (CFD) mesh

The notes' typed set (NOTE-7, NOTE-10) on every CFD engine (`engines/cfd_requirements.py`, rulebook 63).

| ID | State | Rule |
|---|---|---|
| CFDREQ-1 | in force | **One seam.** The builder reads the notes once per flow attempt, before any engine plans (`agents/builder/invoke._read_flow_requirements` -> `cfd_requirements.read_for_attempt`, route "flow"), keeps the reading in the workspace and says it ("From your notes: ..."). Every CFD engine reads the set through `cfd_requirements.requirements_for(workspace)` (an `Ask`) where its own plan is made: snappyHexMesh's external and internal drivers, cfMesh's meshDict writer, the multi-region mesher's configure step, VMTK's configure step, the gmsh flow driver and the snap-grid driver. Each engine records what it did with each requirement beside the case (`cfd_requirements_plan.json`) and says it before meshing ("On this mesher, ..."); a requirement it has no setting for is "not applied on this mesher", told, never silently dropped. **Netgen and the wrap rung read it too** (rulebook 68): Netgen's plan (`engines/netgen/driver.plan_case` -> `cfd_requirements.netgen_inputs`) takes a size at the walls, a global size as a band, the smallest and largest edge as clamps on its passage-sized edges, a cell count as its budget (a target also refines toward it, a count under the floor coarsens below it, said), the layer count, first layer or y+ (the widest opening as the reference length), stack and growth; a growth ratio and a size in one named place are not settings it has, said. A mesh built on a thinner rung of its ladder says so on the layer rows (`netgen_built`). On the CFD wrap rung (LAD-cfd-5) each engine applies the notes to the wrapped surface as to any surface, and every size row says so with the wrap's distance from the CAD (`wrap_rows`); on its last step (gmsh filling the wrap's own triangles) a size at the wall is the nearest - the wall keeps the wrap's triangles - and no wall layers are built, said. The wrap's own distance is set by the geometry, never by the notes. |
| CFDREQ-2 | in force | **Sizes are snapped to what the engine builds, and the size built is said.** snappyHexMesh: a global (or largest) size is the background cell, the box cut in whole cubic cells and the levels re-counted on it so the wall keeps its absolute size; a size at the walls (or the body's own size in a named place) is the wall's level, round(log2(background / size)) - with no background size of the user's, the background is nudged (under 1.42 times either way) to the wall size times a whole power of two, so the walls land on the size asked; the smallest cell caps every level; a size on a named region or opening is that region's level; the wake's size or "finer" is its levels under the wall; a growth rate is nCellsBetweenLevels = ceil(ln 2 / ln rate). cfMesh: maxCellSize, the wall's localRefinement cellSize (cfMesh lands on max / 2^n: a wall size alone nudges the background so the walls land on it; with a background size too, the nearest halving, said), a wake box held to a quarter of the budget, a named patch's own cellSize, a wake box along +x, the smallest cell as a floor on every refinement. The multi-region mesher: the background cell and the regions' levels. VMTK: clamps on its radius-adaptive edge length. gmsh flow: the body / fill element size, its smallest and largest. The snap grid: its background cell and growth. VALIDITY IS NEVER TRADED: snappy's body-sealing level, an internal passage's 8 cells across, an opening's level that keeps it open, a thin region's level that keeps its zone - a size past them is the nearest that holds, said. Our accuracy rules give way (a coarser size than our rule is built, and said "results may be less exact"). A background past half the largest machine is the nearest that fits, said. |
| CFDREQ-3 | in force | **A cell count the user sets wins over ours, up to the largest machine** (Areen, 14 Nov: "they specify whatever they want, it's up to them completely"): on snappyHexMesh and cfMesh a count past the standard machine's `settings/policy.CELL_HARD_LIMIT` is planned as asked and runs on a bigger machine (RUN-9), said in its row ("... more than our standard machine is sized for, so it is meshed on a bigger one"); the other engines hold it to the standard machine, said. The user's count is the plan's limit too: our gap boxes (PLAN-cfd-1) never raise a budget past it. A ceiling or a target is the engine's budget (snappy's maxGlobalCells and the size brake's plan, the multi-region mesher's, VMTK's, the snap grid's; cfMesh coarsens the wall one octree level at a time until the case's own estimate fits); a floor raises ours. Past the largest machine it is the nearest, said. A budget of ours gives way to the user's sizes (snappy raises its own to what they need, up to the largest machine, with a note); the user's own count comes first over their sizes, said. gmsh's flow mesher sizes by element size only: a count there is measured and told. |
| CFDREQ-4 | in force | **Boundary layers as asked.** The count replaces the planner's (its 3-5 / 3-6 is ours); a tight non-orthogonality of the user's is held while layers are added, and when that takes layers back the layers row says so; snappyHexMesh takes a first layer height, a stack thickness or both in absolute sizes (relativeSizes false; minThickness a tenth of the thinnest layer, minVol under the thinnest layer cell), a growth as expansionRatio; cfMesh takes the count, thicknessRatio and maxFirstLayerThickness (no stack thickness: told); VMTK the count and the growth (sublayer ratio; absolute thicknesses: told); gmsh flow and the snap grid have no prism layers: told. A y+ is a first layer height when the flow's numbers are known: Re = U L / nu (air 1.516e-5, water 1.004e-6 m²/s at 20 °C, or the user's), Cf = 0.026 Re^(-1/7), y = y+ nu / (U sqrt(Cf / 2)) - or from a Reynolds number and a length alone; L is the notes' length, else the reference length the user confirmed, else the body's (or bore's) length, said. With a number missing nothing new is asked: the missing one is named and the layers keep our sizing. A first layer the user gave is used as given. |
| CFDREQ-5 | in force | **Quality limits tighter than ours go to the mesher where it has the setting.** snappyHexMesh's meshQualityControls take a tighter maxNonOrtho (the layer stage's relaxed bar 2 degrees under it), maxInternalSkewness (and maxBoundarySkewness, and the relaxed one), minDeterminant and minVol; ours stay where they are tighter (said). cfMesh: a non-orthogonality under its own edge (75) sends a mesh past it through its optimiser and a protective re-mesh (`engines/cfmesh/rescue.near_edge`). Every other limit is measured on the mesh, never promised. |
| CFDREQ-6 | in force | **Asked against delivered.** When the manifest is written (`engines/manifest.write_manifest` -> `cfd_requirements.attach`) every requirement gets a row: limits measured cell by cell on the OpenFOAM polyMesh (`engines/cfd_cell_measures.py`: checkMesh's own non-orthogonality, skewness, aspect ratio, a face-area determinant, volume, Fluent's orthogonal quality; up to 6 million cells) with the share past the target and the outer limit, else against the worst figure the engine reports; the cell count against the built count; the layer count against the coverage (80%) or the layers built; a first layer height or a y+ turned into one against the height the mesher's dictionary was given (within 1%) and the layers it grew (the same 80% of the wall) - met, or not met with both numbers; the wall's refinement level against the level the dictionary carries ("level 3 at the walls (cells of 6.25 mm at the walls), not 4: ..."); an ask the reading's checks set aside is a row too ("not applied - <why>"), so an attempt whose only ask was set aside is never silent; the mesh report lists every one as a "You asked: ..." row beside the checks, as the FEA report does; sizes as the setting built (the largest cell measured). Each measured row is a row of the verdict's one list of limits (`verdict_limits.inputs_for`: "You asked: ..."), a missed one a note on a delivered mesh - never a failed run, never told as met. The closing message carries one line ("From your notes: ..."). |
| CFDREQ-7 | in force | **Nothing said = identical.** With no notes, or notes that ask nothing of the mesh, no Ask exists: every engine's plan, dictionaries, budget and mesh are byte-identical to before, no plan record is written, and the quality record and the verdict's rows are unchanged. A plan that carries the conditions step's answer (CFDREQ-8) has said something: its first layer is sized from it. |
| CFDREQ-8 | in force | **The flow conditions the user confirmed size the first layer** (rulebook 73, `contracts/flow_conditions.py`). The intake's conditions step asks the fluid, its speed and the wall treatment, by button or in the user's own words; a click is never a note (NOTE-1), so before this rule the answer reached only the plan's prose and the first layer kept the planner's own relative size. The plan now carries the answer as ONE fixed sentence ("Fluid: water at 20 °C at 1 m/s at the inlet. Near the walls: a resolved wall (y⁺ ≈ 1); the first layer is sized for y⁺ ≈ 1 at that speed."), written and read back by the same module; the run reads it (`cfd_requirements.with_conditions`, in `read_for_attempt`) into the same set a y+ typed in the notes makes: a resolved wall is y+ 1, wall functions y+ 50 (inside their 30-300 band, with room for slower regions); the speed in m/s (km/h, mph, knots; a Mach number for air at 343.2 m/s; a volume or mass flow through the one inlet whose size is known); air or water give the viscosity (CFDREQ-4). Every engine then sizes the first layer as for a typed y+ (snappyHexMesh, cfMesh, Netgen) or says it has no layers (gmsh flow, the snap grid) or no absolute layer setting (VMTK). The row says where it came from ("(from your flow conditions)"). The user's own notes and settings win: a layer count of 0, a first layer height or a y+ of theirs is used and the line says the wall treatment's is not - and a y+ of theirs with no speed or fluid of its own is sized at the conditions' speed and fluid (a y+ typed as the answer to the conditions question is read as a note too: job 63b0fd25's "y+ of 1 at the wall" was never turned into a height and kept relative layers of about y+ 50). A speed that cannot be turned into m/s or a fluid with no viscosity here is said on the plan ("the first layer keeps the mesher's own sizing, because ...") and on the row; nothing is guessed. A plan with no such sentence (the model's own words) is unchanged - but a summary the model redoes in its own words after the conditions were answered (a note at the plan card: "use snappyHexMesh") gets the sentence back at submission (`agents/intake/flow.with_conditions_sentence`; job 9a7fcd14 lost it and sized relative layers). **An OK on the proposal is no ask** (round 4): the plan says what the user only accepted ("Left to the application's proposal, which the user accepted: ..., the wall treatment."; `flow_conditions.accepted`), and wall functions only accepted size nothing - the mesher's own relative layers stay, as before this rule, and the plan says so ("the layers keep the mesher's own sizing, which it grows on the whole wall"): an absolute y+ 50 first layer was about as thick as the wall cells (1.35 mm under 1.72 mm; 0.99 mm under 2.35 mm) and grew on 4-6% of the wall (jobs 07fd516d, 8eec5b7b, c0b45ad1). A wall treatment the user chose (typed, or the "Resolve the wall" button), and a y+ or first layer of their notes, stay asks (CFDREQ-9 makes a thin one the mesher keeps). |
| CFDREQ-9 | in force | **A first layer far thinner than the wall cells is one snappyHexMesh keeps** (rulebook 73, `engines/cfd_requirements.layer_bridge`, `_layer_determinant`). A resolved wall (y+ about 1) on a large part asks a first layer hundreds of times thinner than the wall cells, and snappyHexMesh took every layer back: job f4019933 (a 1.66 m pipe reducer, water at 1 m/s, a 0.022 mm first layer under 4.4 mm cells) was delivered with 0% of its wall covered. Its own log, replayed on the delivered mesh, names two checks: the cell determinant (about 27 (thickness / width)^2 for a flat cell, so a layer that thin reads 0.0007, under the 0.001 bar - 633,244 faces) and the face weight between the last of 5 thin layers and the 4.4 mm cell above it (under 0.02 - 67,540 faces). Now, when a first layer the user asked (a height, or a y+ turned into one) is more than 50 times thinner than the cells the layers grow into: (1) the layer stage's determinant bar (`relaxed minDeterminant`) sits at half what so flat a cell reads (faces up to twice the cell wide), never above ours, never under 1e-6, never with a determinant limit of the user's, and unchanged when the layers clear our bar already; (2) on the internal case, with no layer count of the user's, the stack gets the layers that fill the thickness our own plan gives the wall (its layers growing to a last layer 0.3 of the cell, about 1.1 cells) from the asked first layer, growing by the same rate - at most 25, and at most what half the pass's budget holds on the wall's faces (its area over the cell); past that the count stops and the first layer is the thinnest that count still fills the stack with, said with the y+ it gives. The row says the layers and why ("21 layers (not our usual 5) ..."). The cells the layers grow into are the near-wall band's (one level finer than the wall's), which is what the internal case now hands the dictionary. Replayed on job f4019933's own mesh (OpenFOAM 11, layer stage only): 5 layers 0%, 21 layers 0% (the determinant), 5 layers with the bar 0.1% (the face weight), 21 layers with the bar 100% of the wall, 21 layers 5.05 mm thick, checkMesh OK, non-orthogonality 45, 4.37M cells. A first layer the cells keep already (job 9c638f7a's rotor: 0.027 mm under 1.25 mm, 46 times, 81.6% kept) writes the same dictionary as before. The external and multi-region cases get (1) only: their size brake does not price more layers yet. **The thick side** (`layer_fit`, every snappyHexMesh case): a first layer the user asked so thick that the plan's layers would stack it past the thickness our own plan gives the wall gets as few layers as stay inside it, at least one ("1 layer (not our usual 5): from a first layer that high our usual 5 would make a stack 10.03 mm deep under 1.716 mm wall cells, more than the mesher can grow; 1 fills the 1.847 mm our plan gives the wall"); a first layer thicker than that whole stack is one layer of the stack's thickness, the nearest, said with the y+ it gives. Measured cases: a typed y+ 50 layer, 1.348 mm under 1.716 mm cells, 5 layers grew on 5.7% of the wall (job 07fd516d) - now 1 layer; 0.989 mm under 2.352 mm, 4.18% (c0b45ad1) - now 2. Not yet re-run on the meshes. **The room counts the wetted wall** (round 8): a hollow part is staged with its whole metal skin as the wall, and snappyHexMesh puts faces on the bore alone, so the faces the room is spent on are the wall pieces with the cavity on one side (`engines/passage.cavity_skin`, the test cfMesh's bore staging uses); the whole wall when none is kept (a fluid domain) or the test cannot run. s_duct_004: whole skin 235,911 faces, wetted 113,680 (snappy made 101,205): a y+ 1 ask goes from 15 layers (0.036 mm, y+ 1.72) to 18 (0.0207 mm), the extra layers inside the same half of the budget. Read only when a bridge is asked. |
| CFDREQ-10 | in force | **An engine that can set what the notes ask** (rulebook 73, `engines/cfd_requirements.engine_that_can`, `application/pipeline_run`). The engine is picked for the user. When the notes the request card already read before approval (NOTES-CFD-1, `part_matches.notes_for_run`: no model call), or a resolved wall the user chose in the flow conditions, ask something the picked engine has no setting for (an absolute first layer or a y+ on VMTK, a control whose lane is NotHere) and another engine of this flow's ladder can set all of those, the run meshes with that one, from its own default settings, and the run's engine line says why ("Meshed with snappyHexMesh, because it can set the first layer for the y+ you asked for."). Never an engine the user named (only the system's pick, `suggested_confirmed`, moves; the ladder may still move it after), never a run that asks nothing. An absolute layer height (a first layer, a y+, a stack) goes to the engine of the ladder that delivers it over the whole wall first - snappyHexMesh, then cfMesh (`LAYER_HEIGHT_DELIVERS`): on s_duct_004 a y+ 1 on cfMesh had layers on 78% of the wall, not met; on snappyHexMesh on 100% (m483 round 9). The order only ranks engines of the job's own ladder. |
| CFDREQ-11 | in force | **A limit written for even cells is not judged on prism-layer cells** (rulebook 73, `engines/cfd_requirements._layer_cells_said`, `ISOTROPIC_MEASURES`). An aspect-ratio limit (a structural list's "aspect ratio under 4") on a flow mesh with prism layers is told, not judged: the layer cells are thin by design, their aspect ratio set by the first layer height, and this mesh's figures do not tell them from the rest, so the share past it is information ("... - prism layer cells are thin by design and are counted here; a limit for even cells is told, not judged, on a mesh with layers"), never "not met" (a y+ 1 stack read 43.9% of cells over 4, not met). Measuring it on the non-layer cells alone would need each cell's layer membership from the mesher, which the figures do not carry. A limit the user said is hard is judged on every cell; the CFD measures (non-orthogonality, skewness, determinant) are judged as before. |

| CFDREQ-12 | in force | **A place named by the role the user confirmed** (rulebook 73, `engines/cfd_requirements.openings_named`). A size or refinement in one place on the flow route that names an opening by its role ("the outlet", "outlet 2", "the inlets") is the opening the user confirmed with that role at intake (`port_declaration.json`, the same roles the solver exports read), whatever the CAD called it; then by its name. Several openings sharing the role with no number: all of them, said ("all 2 openings you confirmed as outlets"); a number picks one in the declared order. The confirmed role wins over a name that is another opening's, and the row says which was used. Nothing matches: not applied, the line listing every opening with its role ("p_1 (inlet), p_2 (outlet)"). No roles confirmed: names only, as before. On snappyHexMesh's internal ports and cfMesh's patches (a patch's own type counts as its role). m484x D1, round 13: straight_reducer_002's p_1 / p_2 - "refine around the outlet to 2 mm" was told no opening named 'the outlet'. |
## AIG: the AI never fails or retries a run

The rules plan comes first. An AI change is used only when it is valid and inside the rules' bounds,
and every AI error falls back to the rules. These rules cover the AI steps outside the FEA builder
(AI-fea-n above) and the verdict (VER-5).

| ID | Status | Rule |
|---|---|---|
| AIG-1 | in force | **The rules build when the AI builder gives no mesh.** On cfMesh, VMTK, gmsh (CFD) and snappy multi-region, an attempt whose builder model was down, busy, slow, raised, or ended its rounds without submitting a mesh is built in the same attempt by the rules plan (`engines/rules_plan.py`): the same tools, validation and gates, the layer count the request names, every size from the engine's own defaults. It is never an infrastructure retry and never a failed run. The why record and the run's notes say so. `BUILDER_AI=off` builds every such attempt by the rules plan. |
| AIG-2 | in force | **The AI plan is never worse than the rules plan.** Before an AI builder plan is used, its planned cells (the engine's own estimate of the case configure_mesh just wrote, at the sizes the mesher really makes) are checked against the one-job cell limit. When the AI plan is over the limit and the rules plan (`engines/rules_plan.py`) is not, the rules plan is configured instead (`engines/plan_guard.py`), the builder's tool result says so in plain words with both numbers, and the why record cites this rule. An estimate that cannot be read, an engine with no rules arguments to compare, or a rules plan that fails or is no better leaves the validated AI plan in force. cfMesh carries an estimate; VMTK fits every strategy to the limit itself, the snappy planner holds max_cells to it and the FEA AI is bounded by AI-fea-5. |

## SHELL and SURF: the mid-surface extraction and surface files (rows from SHELL-50 and SURF-14)

The SHELL and SURF families' earlier rules are written in `docs/design/shell/README.md` and cited in `cad/midsurface.py`,
`cad/shell_pairs.py`, `cad/surface_model.py` and `engines/surface_run.py`; from SHELL-50 and SURF-14 each new one also
has its row here.

| ID | Status | Rule |
|---|---|---|
| SHELL-50 | in force | **Two free-form skins a wall apart have the offset of one as their mid-face** (`cad/midsurface.offset_mid`, `offset_skins`, `OFFSET_SKINS`, `OFFSET_OFF_MIDDLE` = 0.10, `OFFSET_VARIES` = 0.15). A pair whose two faces are both open B-splines (or Beziers) - a stamped or drawn panel's skins - gets as its mid-face the smaller skin's offset by half their mean wall toward the other, bounded in that skin's parameters (its outline, or where its outline and the other's overlap, worked out flat as SHELL-9 does). Kept only when every point read on it lies within 5% of the wall of the middle (`abs(dA - dB) / (dA + dB)` at most 0.10: a shell's bending stiffness about it within 3%) and the wall read across it varies by at most 15%; an uneven wall's thickness is then carried point by point (SHELL-9b). An even pair tries it before the overlap of its two half-offsets (a Boolean common of two different free-form surfaces lying on each other: past the 1800 s extraction deadline on 153 x 103-pole skins); a tapered pair tries it after its fitted mid-face is off the middle (17 and 33 midpoints a side miss beads and swages: up to 65% of the wall off). |
| SHELL-50b | in force | **A big even skin pair that is not each other's exact offset is read like a fitted wall** (`cad/midsurface._extract`, `WALL_VARIES` = 0.01). When SHELL-50's offset mid-face of an even pair reads a wall varying by over 1%, the mid-face is marked fitted: bridged to the mid-faces it continues (SHELL-10) and its wall carried point by point (SHELL-9b). |
| SHELL-50c | in force | **A small even skin pair is read exactly as before SHELL-50** (`cad/midsurface.big_skins`, `OFFSET_EVEN_POLES` = 5,000). An even pair takes SHELL-50's offset only when one of its skins has at least 5,000 poles: the overlap of two half-offsets stalled past the 1800 s deadline on 270 x 170, 283 x 203 and 153 x 103-pole stamped skins, and works on small ones. Read as offsets, a hat rail's small bend and joggle skins joined with degenerated edges on the flat faces beside them (4e-6 to 5e-5 mm), which crashed the mesher (SHELL-54) and, removed, still stopped gmsh; read as before, the six hat rails and the shroud whose shell had changed are byte for byte the base's again. Tapered pairs (SHELL-9 then SHELL-50) are not affected. |
| SHELL-51 | in force | **A wall thinner than its tessellation's error is no coincidence** (`cad/shell_pairs.ray_partners`, `COINCIDENT_RESCUE`, `LYING_FLOOR_FRACTION`). The pairing rays read two faces that share no edge and meet under the floor somewhere as lying on each other, and read through them out to the two tessellations' errors added (cad/fea_features COINCIDENT_BAND). On a 1000 x 700 mm floor panel 0.9 mm thick the skins' tessellations were 4.2 and 3.2 mm off their surfaces: they crossed, and all but 20 of the 19,955 rays across its wall were read through. A pair the plain reading nominates that the lying-on reading loses is given back; the exact test on the B-rep then settles it, and a given-back pair whose exact wall is under the floor (`FLOOR_FRACTION` of the diagonal) is no wall ("the faces lie on each other"). A part with no faces read as lying on each other is read exactly as before. |
| SHELL-52 | in force | **No quick inside test past its triangle budget** (`cad/midsurface.quick_triangles`, `QUICK_MAX_TRIANGLES` = 2,000,000; `_Solid(quick=...)`). A body's quick triangulation (within 0.5% of its thinnest wall) is first estimated from the pairing's tessellation: each face's triangles times that tessellation's measured error over the quick deflection (a curved face's count grows as one over the deflection; a flat face's does not), never fewer than it has. A body whose estimate passes the budget is told by the exact classifier alone - the same answers, read slower: the 0.9 mm floor panel asked for a copy meshed within 0.0045 mm (about 200 million triangles; at 0.2 mm it already took 318,000 and 1.9 GB) and the extraction process died. Any other body is read as before. |
| SHELL-53 | in force | **The joins' distance readings skip what a box already settles** (`cad/midsurface.bridges`, `_free_edges`, `_geometry_box`, `_box_gap`, `BOX_PRUNE`). SHELL-10's bridges read every free edge of every mid-face against every other mid-face, and each candidate pair of edges at ten points, with an exact distance each; a mid-face or an edge whose box (read on the exact geometry) lies farther than the reach or the tolerance is not read, and a pair stops being read at its first point past the reach. No point of a shape lies nearer than its box, so every answer is the same; only the time changes (a 1,451-face antenna cover sat in the bridges for over 20 minutes). Also SHELL-53's: a face's area read once (kept by the face itself), and the box test in plain comparisons. |
| SHELL-54 | in force | **An edge with no 3D curve has no length** (`cad/midsurface._length`). A degenerated edge (a point a face's outline passes through) measures 0 instead of being handed to OpenCASCADE's linear properties, which read a curve that is not there: the mesher's edge census (SHMESH-35, `_interior_faces`) of a sewn mid-surface with such edges died in a segmentation fault (a hat rail, crit mode: the mesh child's EOFError at 13 to 21 s). Every edge with a curve measures as before. |
| SHELL-55 | in force | **A wall is read where its faces face each other** (`cad/midsurface._extract`, `EVEN_WHERE_FACING`). When the even-wall test (`shell_pairs.even_wall`, every grid point of the smaller face against the other face's whole surface) fails, the pair reads as a taper, its fitted mid-face cannot be made, and the tapered reading (`shell_pairs.tapered_wall`, only the points whose foot lies on the other face itself) found one thickness within EVEN_SHARE and the faces parallel within PAIR_DEG, the pair is an even wall and its mid-face is the overlap of the two half-offsets. A printed funnel's two coaxial 45-degree cones 7.8 mm apart read "turned 90 degrees" near the spout (feet past the other cone's apex), the taper fit through those points was 35% off its middle, and the funnel had no wall at all. A taper whose fitted mid-face is made, and every other pair, reads as before (applied before the fit, the rule changed the joins of a 1,451-face cover: 31 open edges became 115). |
| SHELL-56 | in force | **A thick region is thick metal, not a speck** (`cad/midsurface._drop_specks`, `SPECK_FLOOR`, `SPECK_WALLS2` = 4). A mixed body's region of thick or solid faces is a thick region only when its surface is at least 4 times the square of the thickest shell wall it touches (its faces' neighbours, else theirs, else the body's thickest shell wall): a boss two walls across and one wall high has about 9. A region under that (a counterbore's seat, a 4 mm land, a pin tip, a dimple) is counted in the body's row as `specks` and is not listed among the regions that stay solid; a body whose regions are all specks reads "shell". Reporting only: the faces' roles, the mid-surface, its joins and the record's checks are made exactly as before. On the board, any floor from 4 to 6 walls squared turns the same 8 of the 50 mixed parts to shell, each a plate with seats, lands or dimples in its render. |
| SHELL-57 | in force | **A wall region is as wide as its steps let it be** (`cad/midsurface._region_steps`, `REGION_STEPS`). SHELL-19 judges a wall whose thickness changes against its whole region's width (`union_width` of its CAD faces); the region's width is now read with its STEP faces in it: a face that meets two or more of the region's faces, no two of them the two sides of one wall (that is a rim), no wider than the region's thickest wall, and the side of no wall unless that wall is a sliver read across (narrower than it is thick: a 0.5 mm rib's two sides). A printed lid's 3.5 mm floor with 4 mm ribbed bands read 5.5 mm wide ("a bar or a block") and was left solid; it now reads 18 mm wide and is a shell. A region without step faces reads as before. |
| SHELL-59 | in force | **A shell the mesher could not mesh across its dropped slivers is read again with them** (`engines/shell_run.run`, `SLIVERS_AGAIN`; `cad/midsurface.extract_file(slivers=False)`, `has_slivers`). SHELL-16 drops a sewn face narrower than a tenth of its wall and sews its neighbours across the gap at 1.5 times the widest sliver. On a hat rail with a joggle (cen_s0111) ten slivers up to 0.23 mm wide went, the body was sewn again at 0.34 mm, and the mesher failed ("Knots interval values too close"); read with its slivers kept the rail meshes (7,835 elements, valid). When the mesh fails with a ValueError on a surface SHELL-16 sewed (after SHELL-26's retry without short plates, when that applies), the part is extracted again with its slivers kept and meshed once more, and the record says so (`slivers_kept`). A part the mesher meshes the first time is made exactly as before; dropping slivers less often instead (only those under 0.033 walls) was measured on the 31 board parts that drop them: one fixed, one worse, five meshes 2 to 5 times larger. |
| SHELL-60 | in force | **What a shell leaves out is measured** (`cad/midsurface._measure_left_out`, `_thick_measure`, `THICK_MEASURE`, `THICK_POINTS` = 6,000, `THICK_MEASURE_S` = 30). Each thick region of a mixed body (after SHELL-56) gets its size (its faces' box, mm, largest side first, `size_mm`) and about how much metal it holds (`volume_mm3`): the points of a grid over the regions' boxes, grown by the body's thickest shell wall, that lie inside the body and more than half a wall from every sewn mid-face - metal no shell element stands for - each counted for the region whose box is nearest. A body with no shell on a part that has one gets its size. The work is counted, never timed: past 60,000 exact readings a part (`THICK_WORK`: each point's inside test and each distance to a mid-face) its regions keep their size and no metal (the words then give their surface), so the record is the same on any machine under any load. Reporting only: the mid-surface, its joins and the record's checks are made exactly as before. Built parts: a 30 mm cube boss on a 4 mm plate reads 27,729 mm3 (27,000 true), a 16 mm rod 250 mm long under a plate 47,791 (50,265 true); a 60 x 60 x 10 mm pad reads 50,660 = the pad (36,000) and the plate under it (14,400), which has no mid-face there either. |
| SHELL-61 | in force | **The mid-surface is a function of the CAD and the settings, not of the machine** (`cad/midsurface.extract`, `_OneThread`, `ONE_THREAD`; `JOIN_TIMED`, `CLOSING_TIMED`, `PLATE_PASS_TIMED` = False; `_join_failure`; `_clock_decided`; TIME-1). cen_s0094 gave two mid-surfaces from one tree: its general join (SHELL-3) takes a time that moves with the load (206 s on a lab machine at load 35, 299.1 s on another at six jobs; the whole extraction 152 s alone) against SHELL-11's 300 s stop; inside it the general join is kept, past it the straight join and its closing passes stand - so a board run on a loaded machine and a single-part run on a quiet one made different shells (384 s and 152 s). Three more clocks decided silently: the SHELL-23 plate passes were skipped with under 1.5 x the time spent left in the work budget, the SHELL-30 and SHELL-22 closing passes with under 90 s left, and a general join was not started (or got a shorter stop) with little budget left. Now none of them reads the clock: each pass runs whenever it is called for, and the work budget's own stop (SHELL-18, said) and the extraction's deadline are guards. The extraction also runs with OpenCASCADE's thread pool held to one thread (its parallel tessellation had left 15 to 33 worker threads in the process the general join is forked from), and the extraction's own process starts numpy's BLAS / OpenMP on one thread (`child_environment`, `SINGLE_THREAD_ENV`: they had started 15 threads during wall pairing), and a general join that could not be made is said in the record ("its process stopped without a result"), never silent. SHELL-11's stop stays at 300 s as deployed (`JOIN_DEADLINE_S`) and still decides on a few parts (s0094, r0031, r0228, r0527, s0084, fr018): a 1,200 s guard, which only a join that never ends would meet, pushed r0031, fr018 and s0084 to 1,933 - 2,593 s, past production's 1,800 s extraction deadline, so it waits for the general join's work budget (with JOIN_CLOSE_FIRST). Each trip is said in the record and recorded in the run (`engines/determinism.note_time_limit`, where "shell_extraction"), so the part's non-repeatability is visible. A CANDIDATE join choice is in the code, OFF until the whole board has been compared (`JOIN_CLOSE_FIRST`, `_closed_first`, `_closed_clean`, `SLIVER_EDGE_WALLS` = 0.02): the straight join's closing passes are made first, in a forked child (sewing widens the tolerances of the shapes it is given, in place), and the closed straight join is kept without a general join when it is clean - nothing unjoined, no edge under 2% of the thinnest wall, a shell body's metal within VOLUME_BAND, and (for now, `CLEAN_ON_OWN`, `ON_OWN_WALLS` = 0.01) every face a wall owns on that wall's own surface (a channel's closing strip owned by its web in a flange's plane cost the grid rule 14 of 16 gridded faces); otherwise the joins are chosen exactly as before. On cen_s0094 it gives the 79-triangle shell in 66 s; on a spool its closed straight join has 4 edges under 0.01 mm and 19% more metal, so the general join is kept, byte for byte (closing first without that test made the spool worse: 39,657 -> 46,595 mm2). |
| SHELL-62 | in force | **A thick region holds metal** (`cad/midsurface._drop_bare`, `BARE_FLOOR`, `BARE_DEPTH` = 0.1; after SHELL-60's measure). Faces read as thick or solid make a thick region only when metal lies behind them that no shell wall carries: a region whose measured metal is under a tenth of the thickest shell wall it touches times its surface is counted in the body's row as `bare`, not listed among the regions a shell leaves out, and a body left with none reads shell. On the board four perforated plates (r0001, r0002, r0043, r0051: 3 to 9 regions, 42 to 721 mm2 each) and a straight tube (syn_tp_straight_02) held 0 to 1.7 mm3: their cut-outs' and ends' side faces, read thick. A shell told the user those regions were left out, and the shell + solid split rightly found "no thick region is left once the shell walls are taken out". A region whose metal was not measured stays thick. Reporting only, as SHELL-56: roles, mid-surface, joins and checks as before. |
| SHELL-63 | candidate | **The general join stops on its own signs, never on the clock** (`cad/midsurface._join_general` `tol_cap`, `JoinTolerance`, `_join_tolerance`, `JOIN_EARLY_TOL`, `JOIN_STOP_AFTER`). A fuse that already widened a tolerance past SHELL-12's line (half the thinnest wall) stops before its re-sew - SHELL-12 would turn it down after it, a sew never narrows a tolerance - and is said as SHELL-12 says it; a way turned down for that is not tried again with the same walls (offsets re-made or not: a hat rail's way without re-made offsets was kept after two re-made ways were turned down), and once a way could not be made (its fuse failed, its process died) no other way is tried; each said. With JOIN_CLOSE_FIRST the straight join's closing passes, made in their child, are the straight join's passes when it stays (never paid twice). Measured on the four slow board parts (r0031, fr018, r0228, r0527) before it is turned on. |
| SHSTAGE-9 | in force | **A shell says what it leaves out** (`cad/shell_words.left_out`, `left_out_words`, `left_out_short`, `left_out_lines`; `application/shell_check.summarise`, `not_shelled`, `shell_view`; `engines/shell_run.words`, `run`; `engines/shell_delivery.results`; `engines/fea_review.shell_facts_text`; `engines/shell_places._why_not`). No solid is made beside a shell (SHSTAGE-7), so a mixed body's thick regions and a body with no shell have no elements in a shell mesh; the stage, the run's words, the result and the decks said they "stay solid". Now the stage's reason line, the head row of its list, the declaration settled at Proceed, the run's words, the result's summary, every deck's header (Nastran, CalculiX, Abaqus, Ansys) and the reviewer's facts say what is not in the mesh, with its share of the part's volume (SHELL-60) and, for one region, its size, and the way on, in two short sentences: "1 thick region (about 30 x 30 x 30 mm, 26% of the part's volume) is not in this shell mesh. For a mesh that includes it, ask for a solid mesh." (several: "3 thick regions (26% of the part's volume) are not in this shell mesh. ..."); the decks add one line per region with its size and share. Each row of the stage's list says the region "is not in this shell mesh" with its size and share; the console's one line says "N regions left out" and a mark "not in the mesh". SHELL-6's open places are said to be open (no route meshes them solid), SHELL-26's short plates "left out of this shell mesh". A part with nothing left out says nothing new. What a user gets is unchanged: a shell on such a part is still delivered without its thick regions until a shell + solid mesh is offered for it. |
| SURF-14 | in force | **A closed skin with no wall in it is the part's skin** (`engines/fea_driver.drive`, `engines/surface_run.closed_skin_check`, `closed_skin_no_wall`, `closed_skin_words`; `cad/surface_model.read(closed_skin=...)`, `classify`, `CLOSED_SKIN_WHY`). A file with no solid whose surfaces close is read as the solid they bound (MESH-1). When the user asked for a shell and the mid-surface extraction of that solid finds no wall a shell could stand for in any body (every body solid, "no wall of it is a plate": a 2 m boat hull whose only "wall" is the 55 mm across its inside), the surfaces themselves are meshed as a surface part (SURF-3) at the user's thickness (SURF-4) or none (SURF-5), told in one line ("The file's surfaces close round a space with no wall in it, so they were meshed as the part's skin, 2 mm thick."). A closed skin whose solid has a shell wall (a solid written as its surfaces) keeps its extracted shell; open surfaces, and runs that did not ask for a shell, are read exactly as before. |
| SURF-15 | in force | **A surface file is cleaned and meshed in millimetres, whatever unit it was staged in** (`engines/surface_run.main`, using `engines/shell_run.in_millimetres` and `MM_COPY`, as SHELL-18m does for a solid). The FEA driver stages the upload in metres and runs the surface "staged"; the clean-up (SURF-8..13) and the shell mesher are built and proven on millimetre geometry (every lab and scoreboard run hands them millimetres). The staged surface is written scaled to millimetres (staged_mm.brep, faces in the same order) and the run takes it at 1 mm per unit; its files are in metres as before. Before: a stamped dome (s27_dome_long_r4) read in metres had one of its two 0.5 mm gaps closed by filling a hole with a new face where the millimetre reading moves or trims an edge, and the mesher left the dome's main face bare: the website made 5,411 elements covering 80% of the part (MESH-1c) where the lab made 6,532 covering 99.7%; the website's path now makes 6,507 covering 99.75%. Test: `tests/unit/engines/test_surface_run.py::test_a_surface_staged_in_metres_is_cleaned_and_meshed_in_millimetres`. |
| SURF-16 | in force | **Loose debris is left out of a surface part, and said** (rulebook 79; `cad/surface_model.without_debris`, run by `read` before the clean-up; words in `engines/surface_run.clean_words`). A sheet of a surface file that is not its largest sheet, smaller across (the diagonal of its faces' box) than one element of the mesh - the user's size when they asked one, else the run's own (`surface_mend.run_element_mm` of the largest sheet, the size SURF-12 closes gaps by) - and with no other sheet's free edge within a tenth of its own size (`surface_clean.GAP_FACE`: else it is a patch cut off by a gap that SURF-8 may join - a hole's wall written a hair off its rim), is left out before anything is cleaned, sized or meshed, when all such sheets together cover less than one element's square. The user reads one line: "Left out 8 loose faces (4 stray pieces, the largest 0.42 mm across, 0.36 mm2 in all): smaller than one element of this mesh (34 mm), they are stray bits of the export, not part of the sheet." (record: `cleaned.loose`). A sheet as large as one element, or as large as a size the user asked for, is kept and meshed as before; a file of one sheet reads as before. Why: SURF-5 sizes each sheet with no thickness by its own size (a wall of its size over 300), and the shell mesher's floor is half the smallest wall of all faces (SHMESH-2, `Mesh.MeshSizeMin`), so a 0.3 mm stray patch set the whole mesh's floor at 0.0005 mm and was itself meshed with about 1,900 elements, some past Nastran's limits - which made SURF-5 mesh the whole part twice more (at 2 and 4 times the wall). Lab, a 405 x 255 x 261 mm, 4 mm sheet-metal bracket written as 64 loose IGES faces with four two-face 0.3 mm slivers (`~/surf_lab/build_part.py`): before, sizing walls 0.004 and 9.19 mm, 22,468 elements of which 7,492 on the slivers, three mesh passes, 396 s of meshing; a 231-face part of that kind ran past SHMESH-21's 15 minutes with no mesh. Test: `tests/unit/cad/test_surface_debris.py`. |
| SURF-17 | in force | **Making the surfaces meet stops by the clock** (rulebook 79; `cad/surface_mend.MEND_LIMIT_S` = 300 s, TIME-10's bound on the other readings made before meshing; `_mend`; words in `engines/surface_run.clean_words`). SURF-12 changes the surfaces one place at a time; once 300 s have gone, no further place is started: the places not yet tried are left to the stitching (SURF-8) and to the mesh's own closing (SURF-13), exactly as places SURF-12 could not close; the record keeps `mend.stopped_s` and `mend.untried`, and the user reads "Changing the surfaces to make them meet did not finish in 5 minutes, so 7 places were not tried: they are stitched or closed in the mesh where they can be. The mesh goes ahead." Before, it had no limit: 1,287 s on a 231-face, 4 mm sheet-metal IGES before any meshing started. Its waste is cut first, with the same result: each surface's open places are searched once per mend (`_open`, a memo; the same search ran again for each face of each place tried, each test of a candidate change and the counts before and after it - 160 searches on the lab bracket, 32 after), and a strip's kept piece is sectioned only against the faces the whole strip met (`_cutters(among=...)`). Test: `tests/unit/cad/test_surface_debris.py`. |
| SURF-18 | in force | **A file with no solid waits for its surface reading, and a reading that never came says why** (rulebook 87; `application/surface_check.reading_pending`, `unread_why`, `READING_RULE`, used by `application/fea_check.blocker` and `fea_view`, `api/v1/geometry` through `fea_check.check_live`). A STEP, IGES or BREP file the scout found no solid in is read as a surface (SURF-1) with the FEA measurement, minutes after the scout. Until that measurement is stored and while the check still runs (status pending or scouted), the stage and the confirmation say "This file has no closed solid, so it is being read as a surface to mesh as a shell. This can take a few minutes; Proceed opens when it is done.": Proceed waits (the confirmation refuses with those words), no stop and no way out is drawn, the check line says "No closed solid - reading it as a surface", and the shell fold says it is reading the surface (never "you can proceed now"). When the measurement ended with no reading (it was skipped, too big, timed out, or the faces could not be read), the stage says "This file has no closed solid, and it could not be read as a surface to mesh as a shell: <what stopped it>." with the way on (upload it again, or as one closed solid). Before, both cases said "shell meshes for surfaces like this are coming" and asked for a closed solid, for a kind of file that is meshed as a shell; a triangle file that is an open surface keeps those words (it is not meshed as a shell). |

## Reserved families

These ID families are reserved. Their rules are written by the PRs that build them, and no code
may cite them before then.

| Family | For | Written by |
|---|---|---|
| `PLAN-<engine>-n` | the numbers of each engine's computed plan (`PLAN-fea` above is the FEA path's) | D4 to D8 |
| `LAD-<engine>-n` | each engine's fixed ladder of approved levers | D4 to D8 |
| `SHELL-n`, `SHMESH-n` | shell meshing: wall pairs, shell or solid, mid-surfaces and their sizing (`docs/design/shell/README.md`); since SHELL-23 every plate-like wall is a shell when the user asks for one (the 10 walls of SHELL-2 are one line of advice, not a gate) and SHELL-24 makes a short tapered plate (a rolled flange) one flat plate at its mean thickness; SHELL-25 fits a drafted wall two walls wide; SHELL-26 makes the shell without its short plates when the mesher cannot close them; SHMESH-51 (rulebook 71) decides the rings round a hole by what the hole is - a bolt hole (it lines up with a hole in another plate, it is one of a pattern of alike holes, or its plate is joined to another) gets two rings of quads where they fit inside the user's edges and the room round it, else one, with the reason recorded and told; SHMESH-51b to 51e (same rulebook) plan a gridded face's grid and its two rings together - the grid comes first, each box sized for its band, bands with unequal sides, one ring per hole that cannot have two, and (51e) no grid line ends on a two-ring box's side: it stops at the last line before the box, the box's sides hold the band's count and the cells beside take it; SHMESH-51f/51g (same rulebook): a pass gathers its faults before the model is meshed again (grid cells past the limits with a refusal), keeps the cells it frees by keys that outlive gmsh's tags across redraws and restarts that cannot touch them, counts bolt-hole rounds per face and never lets one face's refusal send another face's holes to one ring; a gridded face refused after meshing for elements past the solver's limits or over the user's longest edge, all in or beside two-ring boxes, sends only those holes to one ring; SHMESH-51h (same rulebook): such a round puts every fault it can on a hole at once (bands not made, elements past the limits or over the longest edge, and against the free mesh triangles and short edges, in or beside a two-ring box), and rounds go on while they send holes (up to 8 a face); SHMESH-51i (same rulebook): a line held back from a bolt box ends on a node of the box's side (whole segments either side, its last piece slanted at most one segment, the slanted cells checked against the solver's limits and the user's quad lines; else it stops one line earlier), so the cells beside the box map; SHMESH-51j (same rulebook): a bolt hole sent to one ring for a fault in its rings keeps its two-ring box (place, size, count; one row out to its two rings' outer circle, where that row is inside the solver's limits, the quad lines and the user's edges), so no line of its face moves; a fault beside a box is put on its hole (a box of its own) in a face's first 2 rounds only, then SHMESH-51b; a face gridded on an earlier pass that is not cut after a round of one ring is refused (SHMESH-51b), never left free without a word; SHMESH-51k/51l (same rulebook): a band's nodes move with the relaxing and mending, and go back to where the band put them wherever a quad of it would come out past the solver's limits or over the user's longest edge; SHMESH-51m/51n (same rulebook): where two joined plates' boxes stand right beside their joint, a line that would land on the joint inside the other plate's two-ring box column lands on a node of that box's side (its last piece slanted; a line the plate only carries moves whole), and a joint with a plate that has no holes is not split there; a chain facing a two-ring box's side takes its count from the box (each piece's class follows), and a 4-segment side is no longer given 6 (its band always failed); SHMESH-51o (same rulebook): a mesh whose two-ring faces carry triangles is made again with one ring on those faces (then on every bolt face) and the one with fewer triangles is kept unless it is worse on the solver's limits or the user's lines; SHMESH-51p (same rulebook): in a face with bolt holes two lines nearer than a tenth of a face size are one line even past their limits; SHMESH-51q (same rulebook): a CAD piece of a hole's own circle is no cell edge at planning (the hole is drawn again as one circle when its ring asks); SHMESH-51r (same rulebook): a side landing inside the other plate's two-ring box column first meets that box's corner, so both chains stay whole; the guard's one-ring trial keeps its faces' bolt boxes owned; SHMESH-51o3/51o4 (same rulebook): that trial is never kept when it loses grids; the first trial is the face with the most triangles of its own near its two-ring holes, alone, then (time allowing) the other such faces; SHMESH-51o5 (same rulebook): a trial is kept only when every face it switches has fewer triangles of its own (those that gain are tried alone once), triangles that move on other faces are told, never taken as the face's; SHMESH-51o7 (same rulebook): a four-sided straight-edged hole the square plan takes is a bolt hole (a carriage bolt's) when it lines up with a hole of about its size in another plate or its inscribed circle is within 15% of a round bolt hole on its plate, and its square ring then takes two rows where they fit; SHMESH-51o8/51o9 (same rulebook): in a face gridded with others along its joints every bolt box owns its sides and keeps its kites under the longest edge; SHMESH-51o10 (same rulebook): a round plate's band round its opening is meshed in rings of the ring's count where the opening's segments stay at half the size or more; SHMESH-51o11 (same rulebook): SHMESH-51b's one ring takes a face's square bolt holes back to one row too; SHMESH-51o12..51o15 (same rulebook): 51b names every face with two-ring bolt holes, the record gives triangles and past-limit elements per face, one ring held for a grid stays in the free face when the grid is refused, and a two-ring box side keeps its count against a class too long for it (the cells beside it go free); SHMESH-51o16/51o17 (same rulebook): a strip one cell from a two-ring box takes its count, and a free four-sided cell of n and n + 2k segments takes rows with three-to-one steps; SHMESH-51o18/51o19 (same rulebook): the guard takes one ring for triangles only where it takes off three quarters of a face's own, and a pass's bolt holes and its faces' 51b one ring go in one start again; SHMESH-51o20 (same rulebook): a class holding a two-ring box side and held elsewhere at another count splits at a transition cell beside the holding curve; SHMESH-51o21 (same rulebook): a grid with no triangle refused for short edges alone, on a face with holes, is kept without a free-mesh trial; SHMESH-51o22/51o23 (same rulebook): transition cells in the faces a grid cuts too, pinned and guarded as bands; SHMESH-48x3 (same rulebook, the user's choice): a grid with no triangle refused for its share under the user's shortest edge comes back when its free mesh has triangles and that line is no worse with it, told in the record; the numbers in the note are placeholders until the rulebook numbers them | the shell PRs 1 to 5 |
| `MIXED-n`, `SHLINK-n` | mixed shell and solid parts: the thick cores, the cut into the walls, the shell-to-solid join, Auto's Mixed, and walls short for their thickness (MIXED-8: a wall reaching under 10 of its walls from its nearest joint or thick region; kept solid until SHELL-23, since then meshed as shell and named in one line of advice) (`docs/design/shell/README.md` 2.6); MIXED-11 (`cad/mixed_split.thickness`, `CORE_THICKNESS`): a left-over piece MIXED-1b turns down (twice its volume over its area under half the thinnest wall) is measured by its deepest point - twice the largest distance from a point inside it to its skin - and is a core when that reaches the floor; a compact lump reads s / 3 by MIXED-1b (cen_s0143's two 10 x 10 x 2 mm pads read 1.05 mm against 1.25), a 0.6 mm ring round a hole still is no core; a piece MIXED-1b takes is taken as before; MIXED-10 (`engines/mixed_run.build`, `WHY_SPLIT`): when the thin walls and the thick regions cannot be cut apart and no wall was kept solid (MIXED-8 advice only), the stop gives the split's own reason, never "walls too thick for shells" (34 of 51 board parts were told that); placeholders until the rulebook numbers them | shell PR 3 |
| `SURF-n` | a CAD file that is a surface, not a solid (rulebook 59; `cad/surface_model.py`, `engines/surface_run.py`, `contracts/shell_thickness.py`, `application/surface_check.py`; `docs/design/shell/README.md` 11). **SURF-1**: an FEA file with no solid whose faces do not close after the clean check's own sewing is a surface part, meshed as a shell without the user saying so, unless its openings span at most 1% of its surface (a closed skin with a gap: stopped as before, with both ways on); the user's words (shell, a thickness) settle a file under the 1% as a surface. **SURF-2**: sewn within the clean check's tolerance only, every free edge left free; a rib's foot is joined to the plate it stands on (three faces at one edge sewn; a foot inside a face joined by scoring that face along it, no face moved); any other edge lying along a neighbouring face within 5 tolerances is counted as not meeting and said, never closed. **SURF-3**: the surface is the mid-surface - the extractor's own record is built from it (a part and a wall per sheet, a mid-face and group per CAD face) and the existing shell path meshes, writes and delivers it as the result; there is no solid to fall back to. **SURF-4**: the thickness is the user's words (a fixed word table; a bare number is millimetres; the latest word stands; one thickness for the file), never guessed; a bare gauge number, a range and a second thickness are said, not applied. **SURF-5**: with no thickness given the shell is still delivered - sized as for a thin sheet unless the notes give a global size (SURF-7) (its size over 300; again at twice and four times that wall while elements are left past Nastran's own limits), every property carrying 0 under a "WALL THICKNESS NOT SET" head - never asked for, never refused. **SURF-6**: what the user reads: one line on the stage and the result ("This file is a surface, not a solid. It is meshed as a shell, 1.2 mm thick."), and no "not clean" for a sheet's own open edges; **SURF-7** (rulebook 61, the combined test branch; `engines/surface_requirements.py`): the mesh requirements in the user's notes (NOTE-7) reach a surface file - the driver reads them BEFORE the surface is meshed, once per run; their sizes, quality limits and element kind are the shell mesher's as on any shell (SHMESH-24..26) and each is told asked against delivered. ONE thickness comes out of the two places one can be read: the word table (SURF-4: the notes, else the stage, else the request) wins whenever it reads one - it is the thickness the stage showed the user before Proceed, and fixed rules read it the same every run, model or no model; the notes' typed set (NOTE-7's thickness) is used only when the word table read nothing and refused nothing (a bare gauge number or a range stays refused); when both read one and they differ, the word table's is used and the other is said as a second thickness the notes name. On a surface file the thickness row reads "met - every shell property carries 1.2 mm" (a solid part's reads "measured from your CAD"). With no notes nothing is asked, cited, said or written (NOTE-9). With a global size and NO thickness the sheet is worked at the size asked - a working wall of the global size, so the shell rules' own floor (half a wall, SHMESH-2) is half the size, the shortest edge the usual criteria list allows - never at SURF-5's thin-sheet guess, and it is meshed once; no thickness is written. **SURF-8** (input stream; SURF-7 is the land branch's): a surface part's gaps are closed up a measured sewing ladder (2, 5 sew tolerances; by rule never past 5 = 1/2000 of the part, the reach SURF-2 already called "does not meet"), every pair of joined edges judged before a rung is kept (two different faces, not lying on each other, the gap within a tenth of the narrower face, no face or other free edge lost); a join that fails is left and said while the others are taken one at a time (pieces of an edge cut at a T-meet traced to their face by where they lie); a stated tolerance never leaves more open than the rule's (the rule's result is kept, said); a larger tolerance is offered only once tried on the file and found to close the gaps; a stitch tolerance in the notes ("stitch gaps up to 0.5 mm") replaces the rule's limit and the face cap, "do not stitch" turns it off; what is open is ONE count whatever tolerance was asked - places where free edges lie along another face within 1/100 of the part, a gap counted once (its pieces and its two sides together) - used by the record, the words and the stated-tolerance check; the words add up ("Closed K of the N places", then N - K left); places within what was stitched are said with why, wider ones with a tolerance only when it was tried on the file and closed them. **SURF-9**: a face written twice (same area, same box, each lying on the other within the sew tolerance) is left out once before anything is sewn. **SURF-10**: an inner loop of a face no bigger across than the gaps being closed (or than the size the notes name: "ignore holes under 5 mm") is filled with the face's own surface. **SURF-12**: on the surface route only, the places SURF-8 counts are made to meet by changing the surfaces, one at a time, nearest first - an edge stopping short extended along its own surface (a natural patch over more of its own surface, a B-spline continued with its own derivatives and kept within a tenth of the sew tolerance of the old, a trimmed face by a strip of its own surface whose sides continue its own edges) and cut where it meets its neighbour, an overlap trimmed back along the neighbour's edge, a step moved onto its neighbour; each change valid, its normal side kept, its area within a strip of the reach, crossing no other face, kept only when less is left open; both faces of a place tried; the cheapest change first (extended or trimmed along its own surface, and only then the edge moved onto its neighbour); a gap is closed when it is narrower than one element - the size the user asked for, else the run's own (its largest sheet's base size) - never past 1/100 of the part, or up to the notes' "close gaps up to 4 mm"; "do not change the surfaces" turns it off; every change told, one sentence per place (what, by how much, where). **SURF-13**: what is still open after SURF-12 and stitching, narrower than the same reach, is closed in the shell mesh (`engines/surface_mesh_close.py`): the free nodes facing each other across the gap joined half way, a node with no partner put into the edge it faces (its element cut in two or three), no node moved farther than the gap; kept only when no element turns or folds and none is past Nastran's own limits that was not before, else the mesh there is as meshed and the place told open with why; "do not stitch" / "do not close gaps" and "do not change the surfaces" turn it off. **SURF-11**: the element count the notes name reaches a surface file (the FEA plan's own reader, asked only when there are words), as it reaches a solid; placeholders until the rulebook numbers them | the surface-files PR |
| `SHSTAGE-n` | the FEA stage's "Mesh as: Auto / Shell / Solid" and the run it settles (rulebook 58): the shell check after the part is measured, Auto's recommendation with its reason (Shell when every body is a shell with no open edge and 90% of the surface thin; Shell + solid only when MIXED-4 and MIXED-8 pass with every wall's reach measured; Solid otherwise), the user's pick or words never overridden, the choice settled at Proceed, and the shell or mixed files made beside the solid deck, which is always delivered (`docs/design/shell/README.md` 7); placeholders until the rulebook numbers them | the shell land PR |
| `SHWRITE-n` | the shell solver files' parts (`engines/shell_writers.py`, `docs/design/shell/README.md` 4). **SHWRITE-1**: every element of a shell (or mixed, or surface-file) deck is in the CAD part (body or sheet) it came from, named as the CAD names it - the STEP names the assembly staging reads into assembly.json (`cad/fea_assembly.read_body_names`, the same names the result viewer lists, `engines/shell_delivery.part_names`), else Part_k. The name is the instance the assembly places when it is the product's own name with an instance number ("PEDESTAL FOOTPAD GUSSET.57", eight gussets of one product keep eight names), else the product's; a name that starts with an assembly's name above it or the file's own name loses that prefix when a real name is left. One property per part and wall, numbered part by part: a part of one property gives it the part's name, a part of several walls one per wall, "<part> t=<wall>mm". Nastran: the part's elements follow its `$HMNAME COMP k"<part>"` line (HyperMesh's component, k its MAT1), each PSHELL is named on the two lines before it (`$ Femap Property i : <name>`, Femap's title; `$HMNAME PROP i"<name>"`, HyperMesh's); Abaqus / CalculiX: an ELSET per part named after it (letters, digits and _, a letter first, unique without regard to case) and a *SHELL SECTION per property; Ansys: an element component per part named after it and a SECTYPE per property named after it. A mixed mesh's solid elements are in their part's ELSET, component and HyperMesh component. The face-pair groups stay as they were; the mesh is not touched (node and element numbers as before); placeholders until the rulebook numbers them | the shell part-names PR |
